###### Programmatic Framework for Unified Governance, Accountability, and Delivery 

###### High-Caliber Leadership Will Create a People-First, High Performance Team 



We’ve assigned **Kevin Dahl as Project Manager and Rick Warner, PE as Deputy Project Manager (DPM), two of our highest-caliber water management leaders** , who will align with MMSD’s management and remain available 24/7. **Kevin will lead day-to-day operations with a steady, proven, hands-on presence. Rick’s Above and Beyond role is a key differentiator: in addition to other responsibilities, we’re intentionally elevating regional partnership and public outreach to the DPM level so these priorities receive dedicated leadership attention** —while also strengthening the cross-functional disciplines that directly support your operations and maintenance teams. In this way, Rick will back up Kevin with selected management needs, so Kevin can stay focused on daily O&M performance and culture building. 



MMSD structured this contract to deliver accountability and transparency, especially for shared costs and maintenance outcomes, across its complex, interconnected system. Jacobs’ program management (PgM)-based operating model will do just that—unifying governance, delivery management, and cost control across MMSD’s facilities. Our PgM framework creates a single source of truth for operating, maintenance, and financial systems and ties performance and cost to verifiable data, shared key performance indicators (KPIs), and disciplined reporting— with dashboards, exception tracking, and structured monthly/quarterly reviews that drive timely corrective action. 

Kevin and Rick will build a **people-first, high-performance culture** grounded in employee development and respect—because strong culture drives safety, retention, and results. Our team has engaged MMSD staff, stakeholders, and union leadership and understands the workforce and

###### IS0-55001-Aligned Asset Management System for Preventive Maintenance (PM)/ Corrective Maintenance (CM) Excellence 



MMSD wants a rigorous and accountable maintenance approach with the visibility needed to manage risk and maximize asset life. **We’ll implement a practical ISO 55001-aligned Asset Management System** , governed through MMSD-facing committees. It will drive reliability-centered maintenance and use NEXGEN as the system of record, with work orders closed using field evidence, supervisor verification, and timely closeout. Shared dashboards will track PM compliance, CM backlog/aging, cost/labor, and asset signals—so performance is auditable and actionable. 

**Maintenance Certainty from Enhanced Leadership and Deep Bench.** As another Above-and-Beyond enhancement, **Jacobs has added a Director of Maintenance & Asset Management, John Loucks-Powell, CRL** —a proven leader who will align the maintenance teams and drive an asset-management-centered PM/CM delivery approach. John is backed by Jacobs’ regional maintenance and specialty resources to support onsite crews during peaks, complex work, and emergencies— so potential staffing constraints won’t impede execution. Jacobs’ national asset management team will also provide tools, analytics, and implementation support to strengthen onsite capability. 

**Reliability-Centered Maintenance to Eliminate Repeat Failures.** We’ll call on our regional and national specialists to also bring their expertise in reliability-centered maintenance, PM optimization, predictive maintenance (PdM) strategies, CMMS/ NEXGEN, and risk-based prioritization to our failure analysis. This ensures we identify true root causes, update work plans and maintenance intervals, prevent repeat issues, and embed lessons learned into ongoing operations. 

**Jacobs’ Closed-loop Work Order Management Approach Will Drive PM/CM Excellence** 

**Reliability Enhancement Programs for Jones Island Water Reclamation Facility (JIWRF) and South Shore Water Reclamation Facilities (SSWRF).** We’ve developed reliability enhancement plans for both WRFs to focus on the assets and systems that have commonly affected uptime. These programs will connect disciplined work execution with reliability analytics to improve equipment availability, reduce unplanned disruptions, and lower associated compliance risk.

###### Elevated Operations Driven by Technical Expertise and Innovation 



###### COST-EFFECTIVE, COMPLIANT OPERATIONS THAT PROTECT MMSD’S REPUTATION 

We’ve listened carefully to MMSD staff to understand your system and what success needs to look like in the future. Our approach is built to protect what MMSD values most: permit performance, zero overflows, Milorganite quality and reliable production, safe and resilient operations, cost effectiveness, and progress toward your 2035 Vision. We’ll operate MMSD’s complex network as one interconnected system—so decisions are coordinated, accountability is consistent, and performance improves across your facilities. 

###### **Technical Excellence, Optimization, and Community‑Facing Performance** 

Jacobs’ comprehensive O&M approach brings deep expertise in asset management, process optimization, digital operations, and system-wide process control. Operating as One Water, One Team, One Vision with openbook reporting and real-time monitoring for MMSD staff, you’ll always have clear visibility into what’s happening, why it’s happening, and what we’re doing about it. 

Additionally, MMSD’s reputation depends on **consistent compliance, reliable operations, and odor management to drive community trust—plus maintaining Milorganite quality** that extends beyond the local community. Our operating plan protects these outcomes through disciplined process control, transparent monitoring, and continuous optimization within a structure that MMSD can audit and govern. 

###### **Commitment to Compliance and “No Surprises” Performance** 

Jacobs will operate every facility and control system to strictly adhere to permit conditions and MMSD’s performance standards—supported by dedicated technical leadership, compliance tools, and a culture of accountability. PgM tools such as dashboards and committee-ready reports will connect operational conditions to outcomes and costs, so issues are identified early, communicated clearly, and resolved with documented corrective actions.

###### EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 









###### **Wet Weather Flow Management to Support MMSD’s Goal for Zero Overflows** 

We’ll apply predictive forecasting, coordinated tunnel and WRF operations, and disciplined decision-making to prevent overflows and basement backups. Our approach emphasizes proactive readiness, resilient wet weather response, and close coordination with MMSD management. **Susan Moisio, PE as Wet Weather and Conveyance Director,** will direct our conveyance experts to help MMSD innovate and address current and future challenges. We’ve also proposed deploying digital tools which provide a real-time view of the network for improved performance. 

###### **Optimization that Delivers Value for MMSD’s Budget** 

**Leveraging our intelligent O&M tools, we have already identified reductions of 20-30% in power and chemical use compared to prior years—efficiencies that are directly reflected in our price.** We’ll continue to target additional optimization opportunities in MMSD’s highest shared cost areas, including power, chemicals, fuel, and biosolids handling, using data analytics, SCADA insights, process modeling, and targeted pilots. Our digital tools that use data science and machine learning have helped achieve 20–30% chemical and power savings for existing O&M clients, such as Wilmington, DE, and West Basin, CA. For example, in taking over the West Basin operations (the largest reuse facility in North America) from your current service provider, we reduced chemical consumption by 15-40% and at Wilmington by 23%. Ultimately, our focus is to continuously optimize cost and risk, while sustaining compliance and performance. 

###### **Milorganite Production: Precision, Continuity, and Brand Protection** 

Milorganite requires precise coordination across multiple processes and an experienced workforce. Our transition and operating approach preserves institutional knowledge and strengthens best practices, safety systems, and process controls to protect continuity and product quality. We’ll track the operating parameters that matter—feeds, dewatering, polymer, dryer energy, and production output—so trend data confirm compliance and reveal efficiency opportunities. 

###### **Odor Control: Prevention, Early Warning, and Documented Response** 

We’ll take a proactive and community-sensitive odor approach that reduces the likelihood of offsite impacts and improves responsiveness—investing in an early warning system with predictive indicators and models to anticipate elevated-risk conditions. If a complaint occurs, we’ll follow a consistent, documented protocol: log, investigate, measure, diagnose root cause, implement and communicate corrective action, and capture lessons learned to prevent recurrence. 

###### ACCESS TO A FULL BREADTH OF TECHNICAL EXPERTISE TO STRENGTHEN OPERATIONS 

By leveraging Jacobs’ vast O&M and technical experts, our delivery model provides both the cost effectiveness and agility of a dedicated local team and the depth of a global enterprise—ensuring continuity, redundancy, and innovation. Through this structure, MMSD gains a high-performing operations partner committed to delivering reliability, operational excellence, and continuous improvement throughout the agreement. **A proof of our commitment is our Annual Innovation Workshop where we bring our industry SMEs directly to your team** to share best practices and new technologies, explore future improvement initiatives and **proactively combat operator complacency over time.**

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 

###### Seamless Transition Will Lay the Foundation for Uninterrupted and Continuous O&M Services 



MMSD’s schedule provides approximately 18 months for pre-term transition, intentionally structured to minimize risk and protect uninterrupted operations. **Jacobs will execute our proven transition approach that delivers continuity, staffing stability, and strong Day 1 performance.** We’ll sequence critical staffing, systems, and governance activities well ahead of the contract start, so **handover occurs without disruption to service, safety, or compliance.** 

**Expert Transition Team, Leadership, and Processes Ensure Day 1 Readiness.** Our transition will be led by **senior transition manager Kelly Irving** and a dedicated team experienced with complex wastewater O&M handovers. We emphasize early engagement, clear communication, and disciplined readiness so people, processes, and systems are fully prepared before operations begin—establishing a strong foundation to elevate performance throughout the contract term. 

**Workforce continuity is central to success. Jacobs brings deep experience transitioning and supporting unionized workforces** , and we will focus on continuing to build and align our relationship with the local Milwaukee unions to ensure success.  In parallel, our PgM team will mobilize during the pre-term period to establish governance, control tools, and management processes—positioning MMSD for a seamless handover on March 1, 2028. 

###### Regional Partnerships and Public Outreach for Community Benefits 



We’re inspired by your vision—and your ongoing partnership and outreach initiatives. We’ll build on your foundation and our partnerships in the region. We’ll explore above and beyond ideas with you within four strategies—community health and environmental enhancement, workforce development and economic growth, sustainability and resilience, and research and development. Our goal is to identify those ideas within each strategy that have the greatest impact on equity, sustainability, and economic growth and bring the most value to MMSD and ratepayers. Examples of these ideas are captured on the next page, and are elaborated upon in **_Section V_** .

###### E. Confidentiality Statement 



**Confidentiality Designations and Legal Basis under Wisconsin Open Records Laws -** Consistent with RFP Section 3.4, we’ve labeled only specific pages of this proposal as confidential where we believe the information involves proprietary “trade secrets” exempt from public disclosure under Wisconsin public records law§ 19.36(5); § 134.90(1)(c). Identified pages include one or more of the following types of proprietary trade secrets: 

- Proprietary value/innovation packages derived from Jacobs-funded investments 

- Client-identifying, client-confidential, or non-public client-specific information 

- Proprietary technologies, tools, methods, processes, and “how we do it” internal frameworks 

- Proprietary reporting tools and processes including custom computer programs, designs, and interfaces 

- Internal pricing, cost approaches, and pricing assumptions 

- Proprietary transition mobilization sequencing, risk controls, and internal tools and processes 

Jacobs derives economic value from these proprietary trade secrets not being generally known, and Jacobs takes reasonable measures to maintain the confidentiality of the information on the identify pages. Public disclosure would likely (1) allow competitors to replicate, reverse-engineer, or underbid Jacobs, causing competitive harm, or (2) reveal details about our other clients that may be regarded as confidential or non-public information. 

Jacobs acknowledges that MMSD will make the final determination concerning any exemption that applies under the applicable public records laws.

## STATEMENT ON STAFFING, TRAINING, AND CORPORATE CULTURE 



###### A. Staffing Strategy and Key Positions 

Our conversations with MMSD staff, stakeholders, and union leadership over the past 2 years have given us a clear understanding of your operations and the condition of your facilities, as well as your expectations for a partnership throughout the contract. Beyond simply meeting permits and maintaining system reliability, you’re seeking a partner that will lead by energizing the workforce, strengthening their sense of belonging, and advancing MMSD’s long-term goals through the focused engagement of a high-performing, collaborative team. We’ve created our staffing approach to meet those needs. It’s designed to deliver real, practical solutions to the staffing, training, cultural, and labor relations challenges you face—and to assure MMSD of the leadership, workforce stability, and operational support you’ll require for long-term success. 



###### 1. ORGANIZATIONAL STRUCTURE 

Jacobs’ organizational structure, shown on **Exhibit II-2,** illustrates our staffing plan that complies with MMSD’s Exhibit E and the pivotal roles we’ve added, which engages some of our top caliber staff to deliver transformative services and outstanding performance. Each leadership position is supported by a team to fulfill the requirements of the contract, and includes staff from within Jacobs, the current O&M employees who will transition to the Jacobs’ team, our subcontractors, and our workforce development, internship, and apprenticeship programs, as presented in **Exhibit II-3** . Staff that represent positions Above and Beyond (AB) the required minimum staffing requirements are denoted on **Exhibits II-2** and **II-3** with the AB icons. **Staff resumes are included at the end of this section on page II-20** . 

Transparency is a cornerstone of our approach. From staffing dashboards to key performance indicators (KPIs) and reporting on maintenance performance, we’ll provide clear, consistent visibility into your operations. This openness will build trust, accelerate decision making, and make sure there are no surprises. Through decades of experience managing utility facilities, including nearly 200 current contracts, and augmented by more than 75 years in wastewater engineering, we understand the responsibility that comes with managing critical public infrastructure. **Our commitment is straightforward: deliver exceptional service from Day 1, while strengthening the workforce, systems, and culture that make MMSD a nationally respected utility.** With a dedicated local Human Resources (HR) team, deep union partnerships, culture of belonging, and robust operations and maintenance (O&M) and consulting bench, we’ll stabilize staffing, accelerate hiring, optimize operations, and build a workforce pipeline through apprenticeships, internships, and career pathways. 

###### **1.1. Leadership Team** 

Project Manager Kevin Dahl will be the direct point of contact for MMSD. He’ll hold regular meetings with his leadership team to set priorities, communicate direction from MMSD, review progress, and complete outstanding action items. Kevin will 

work with his team to see that decisions are made on a predictable schedule, changes are controlled and transparent, and staffing/ performance sync with evolving system needs.

###### 1. OUR HR AND STAFFING COMMITMENT TO MMSD 

###### **1.1. We Bring an Award-Winning Culture Aligned with MMSD’s Values** 

We cultivate a workforce that feels respected, informed, and proud to serve our clients, which we’ll carry forward to Milwaukee. For this project, our culture will promote higher engagement and retention, smoother union relations, clearer communication, and a stronger connection between employees and the community. 

Jacobs consistently ranks among top firms in the industry, including, _Forbes’_ 2025 list of America’s Best Employers—which tracks companies that are navigating unprecedented changes in the business landscape. Jacobs’ culture aligns to published research that indicates employees value compensation, development, and advancement opportunities, meaningful work, supportive colleagues, and workplace well-being and flexibility. (www.forbes.com/lists/best-large-employers)

###### 2. OUR PLAN TO FULFILL AND SUSTAIN THE MINIMUM STAFFING PLAN 

Using our HR model, we’ll staff and retain all 236 positions identified in RFP Exhibit E Minimum Staffing Plan by Day 1, meet Exhibit E’s mandate to fill “Required Positions” within the 90-day grace period, and staff “Key Positions” at all times. We’ll use temporary coverage when needed to maintain performance and review staffing status during governance and the annual joint reviews of the Minimum Staffing Plan. **Exhibit II-6** illustrates our framework for staffing certainty. 

###### **2.1. Retain Qualified Incumbents** 

###### **_Protect knowledge and morale during transition._** 

During the transition pre-term phase, and approximately 60 days before Day 1, an **experienced transition team** will work closely with the unions to provide a stress-free transition to Jacobs. In section " **_3.1 Process for Transitioning Union Employees_** ," we outline how this process will unfold. Our **Transition Manager, Kelly Irving,** will work closely with Brooke and our HR team to **identify the incumbent employees you most want to retain** and prioritize offers and onboarding for those individuals. (See **_Section IV.B Pre-term Transition Activities)_** 

###### **2.2. A Clear Plan to Fill Vacancies—Before They Become an Issue** 

From award forward, we’ll track vacancies daily, launch postings immediately, and escalate hard-to-fill roles to our regional bench for interim coverage until permanently filled. This is how we’ll avoid service risk and fee reductions tied to unfilled roles. 



<!-- Start of picture text -->
I. Key   Key<br>Staff Resumes<br><!-- End of picture text -->

###### **2.3. Staffing for Resilience - Local Core + Regional Bench + National SMEs** 

Our staffing model aims to ensure continuous staffing through four sourcing tactics: 

- 1) Foundational—Current MMSD employees and new Milwaukee-area hires 

- 2) Regional O&M bench – Provides temporary/emergency assignments, specialty skills, and training, as-needed 

- 3) National SME network - Supports the local workforce with process, biosolids drying, odor control, energy, IT/OT/SCADA, asset management, and other specialty needs 

- 4) Staffing contracts - Several temporary employment providers will allow us to meet shortterm needs and potentially convert promising short-term employees to long-term hires should the situation warrant such an approach. 

###### **2.4. Systems and Reporting that Make Staffing Transparent** 

Our HR Information System (HRIS) and compliance workflows will track hiring status, onboarding, certifications, training, and license renewals. We’ll embed staffing metrics (vacancies, time-tofill, retention, training compliance) into the governance and reporting schedule and the annual staffing review. MMSD will know about issues early and decisions will be traceable. 

###### **2.5. Alignment with MMSD’s Broader Goals** 

Our HR systems and practices will support MMSD’s priorities, workforce development, and apprenticeship programs, which will be coordinated with the Workforce Development Plan described in **_Subsection H. Workforce Development Program._** This includes structured career pathways, on-the-job training, apprenticeships in key trades, and internships/co-ops, all of which will be reported annually to MMSD. 

###### 3. UNION AND LABOR RELATIONS 

Led by our Labor Relations Lead, Jeff Williams, we’ve met with several union leaders (AFSCME, IAM, IUOE, Steamfitters, Electricians, and Painters) and their members to introduce Jacobs and our culture. Our early engagement with union representatives has offered insights into what’s important to their union members, such as benefits and apprenticeship programs. We’ll continue this collaborative discourse with unions to foster shared accountability and trust. We’ll continue to build on that during the CBA process and beyond. 

At MMSD, the active CBA will need to be recognized by the successor contractor if all current employees are to be transitioned to the new contractor. As such, the unions will most likely seek to understand what, if any, obligations MMSD will maintain and what obligations will be assumed by Jacobs. **Jacobs intends to honor the CBA and all terms and conditions established therein as far as the law and our agreement with MMSD are concerned.** We’ll work with the unions and MMSD to ensure the interests of all stakeholders are adequately accounted for through the transition. Specific elements of our plan to work with the unions follow and are described in **Exhibit II-7** .

###### **3.1. Process for Transitioning Unionized Employees** 

- Develop a competitive benefits and compensation package that complies with negotiated CBA terms and that the unions will support. Negotiate change in health benefits, retirement, and other aspects of the CBA with the unions to confirm support for changes. 

- Continuously communicate with union representatives and MMSD to encourage partnership and early issue resolution. 

- Consult with management, HR, and the union to help oversee changes. 

###### **3.2. Integrating Union Workforce to Build a Positive Culture from Day 1** 

We’ll communicate with potential employees through the union about our hiring plan, open positions, application procedures, and interview process. Early in the process, our benefits specialists will host workshops to explain our program and answer questions from employees or their family members. Our process for hiring represented employees is outlined below: 

- Notify union of contract start date and our intent to retain represented employees. 

- Request represented employees contact information, classifications, seniority, and training. 

- Request the current union benefits package. 

- Hold union-specific workshops to introduce our management team and answer questions. 

- Notify the union weekly about the hiring process, including the numbers interviewed and the numbers, names, and classifications selected. 

- Following contract award, enter into a successor agreement with the union. 

We’ll schedule periodic labor/management meetings to increase communication, improve safety and productivity, and create a partnering relationship. **Twice yearly, we’ll host a roundtable with MMSD and union representatives to review project performance.** ( **See details on our robust employee transition approach built on decades of experience in** **_Section IV.B. Pre-term Transition Activities_** .) 

- S Jacobs offers exceptional resources and a strong commitment to developing its people. The company emphasizes implementing plant improvements correctly and prioritizing long-term performance and reliability over lowest initial cost. Jacobs is also open to innovative approaches, such as partnering with machinelearning organizations to develop data-driven algorithms that reduce chemical consumption. This approach is why I am proud to be a part of Jacobs’ team. 

   - **Richard Piovoso, Union Lead Operator,** 

   - **Wilmington, DE Wastewater Treatment Facility** 

268_007CAM_4 

###### **3.3. Building the Pipeline with Apprenticeships and Union Partnerships** 

We see an immense opportunity to build apprenticeship programs customized to MMSD’s needs and focused on recruiting to attract and build an industry labor pipeline. Our plans include: 

- Continue to foster union partnerships we’ve formed to engage people from all workforce demographics. 

- Help connect unions to students at the high school and trade school levels, help articulate what membership in a union could do for careers, and encourage students to consider a career path in the trades needed at MMSD’s facilities. 

- Partner with local area chambers of commerce and participate in tradefocused high school technical programs and job fairs. 

- Expand on our internal training programs to include the MMSD-custom apprenticeship model.

###### 4. LONG-TERM RETENTION AND SUCCESSION PLANNING 

###### **Keep People, Grow People, Honor Knowledge:** We’ll preserve MMSD’s 

institutional knowledge and turn today’s team into tomorrow’s leaders. We provide fair pay and benefits, visible career pathways, recognition for certifications and performance, and a culture of respect and inclusion for smoother labor relations and higher retention. As a result, our staff will envision a future at MMSD with Jacobs. Three elements of our retention and succession plan follow. 

- Avoiding disruptions from retirement by hiring and cross-training replacement staff in advance of scheduled retirements, prepping candidates for licensing, and using staff from our regional bench to backfill positions while successors onboard. Our Resource Planning Group will support mentoring, rotations, and leadership workshops to speed readiness for first-time leads and supervisors. 

###### **4.1. Integration: One Team, One Standard** 

We begin by integrating qualified incumbent staff you wish to retain alongside new hires, pairing senior staff with junior operators for skills transfer, and aligning everyone to common performance and safety expectations. Orientation reinforces our code of conduct and a “One Water, One Team, One Vision” culture. Supervisors will conduct regular check-ins to assure people feel respected, informed, and supported. We’ll use the same respectful and transparent approach with labor partners that we’ve used with other unionized workforces, some of which are listed in **Exhibit II-8** . 

###### **4.3. Succession Planning—A Structured, Visible Path to Advancement** 

Our team is structured to provide pathways to advanced positions and fill vacancies as they occur. With a national shortage of certified wastewater operators, proactive succession planning and job marketing is essential. Each department will identify critical roles and designate successors with readiness plans tied to skills, licenses, and leadership milestones. We align these plans with **_Section II.E Technical Training_** and the annual Workforce Development Plan discussed in **_H. Workforce Development Program_** , so training investment is aimed at the roles MMSD needs most. Tools from our Resource Planning Group—career succession planning, leadership development guides, one-on-one career counseling, project manager internship, and leadership-engaged mentor programs—give supervisors and employees a common language and playbook for growth. Elements of the plan include: 

**4.2. Dedicated Team Focused on Attracting, Developing, and Retaining Employees**

- Defined ladders from OIT to licensed Operators and into shift lead/ supervisor roles 

- Analogous tracks for maintenance (mechanical, electrical, instrumentation and controls [I&C], lab, and support functions) 

- Cross-training blocks that deliberately build redundancy and resilience 

- Apprenticeship integration (machinists, steamfitters, electricians, and fleet/auto mechanics) 

- OIT and instrument technician-in-training roles, and internships/co-ops to sustain the pipeline

###### 5. STANDARD EMPLOYEE BENEFITS 

Our comprehensive benefits program supports recruiting, retention, and wellbeing for union and non-union employees. 

###### **5.1. Union Employees—Providing Affordable Benefits, Respect for the Agreement, Clear Communications Commitment** 

**We’ll honor the applicable CBAs for wages, hours, and benefits and engage union leaders through regular labor–management check-ins to maintain clarity on eligibility, enrollment, and leave policies.** Typical benefits found in CBAs for our other projects and to be confirmed with MMSD and the unions include: 

- Medical/prescription, dental, and vision plan(s) and associated employee cost share 

- Retirement programs with any applicable employer matching contributions 

- Paid time off/holidays/leave provisions; short-long-term disability, life, and/or accidental death and dismemberment insurance; leaves of absence language and FMLA coordination 

- Any union-specific allowances or differentials 

- Apprenticeship/education benefits referenced in the CBA 

At Jacobs, our culture of caring and inclusion is foundational to who we are, and we support employees to be their best selves—physically, emotionally, financially, and socially. Our benefits program reflects this. It allows employees to make informed decisions and shape personal benefits, with options for carriers and coverages to suit individual needs. **To maximize affordability, Jacobs discounts health care premiums offered to our union employees and pays 80% of the cost of premiums.** 

###### **5.2. Standard Benefits for Non-Union Employees** 

For non-union employees, Jacobs will provide its standard employee benefits package: 

- Medical, dental, and vision plan options with a range of employer/ employee contributions 

- 401(k) retirement plan and employer match after vesting 

- Paid time off/holidays; short-/long-term disability, life, and/or accidental death and dismemberment insurance 

- Employee Assistance Program (EAP) and well-being resources (see more details below) 

- Licensing/education reimbursement based on identified limits and eligibility 

###### **5.3. Well-Being, Safety, and Leave—Helping Employees Manage Stress** 

Jacobs’ EAP and other mental health resources help employees with work-life balance and stress management. Onboarding materials explain leave policies and wellness resources for union and non-union staff. 

###### **5.4. Enrollment, Employee Communication, and Reporting—Make It Easy, Keep It Transparent** 

We’ll conduct benefits open-house sessions (timed around shifts and with an offsite option), distribute benefits FAQs, and provide individual enrollment support starting at transition. We’ll update information at least annually. HR will track enrollment status and eligibility in our HRIS and include benefits participation and certificationbonus utilization in governance reporting, so MMSD sees how benefits support retention and readiness. 



**Benefts to MMSD: CBA-aligned benefits and clear communication assure continuity and respect for union staff. Competitive, easy-to-understand benefits for non-union and union staff improve recruiting and retention. Covering exam fees and providing certification/degree bonuses will grow workforce skills aligned to MMSD’s goals. Benefits and recognition programs are documented in HR dashboards and reportable to MMSD.** 

###### C. PM and CM Staffing 



Our proactive, reliability-centered maintenance program focuses on in-house delivery and aligns with MMSD’s 85% PM/15% CM goal. We’ll organize people, planning, and materials so crews spend their time on wrenches, not waiting. Our ISO 55001-aligned asset management framework, disciplined planned maintenance optimization model, and CMMS governance ties daily work to measurable outcomes. Dedicated onsite maintenance teams are supported by regional specialists and national experts for surge needs and after-hours coverage. For the full technical approach (asset management, governance, KPIs, and workflows), see **_Section IV.A.iii Maintenance Approach and IV.D Predictive Maintenance (PdM)_** . This section focuses on how we’ll staff to deliver that approach. 

###### 1. MAINTENANCE STAFFING THAT MEETS AND EXCEEDS MMSD’S GOALS 

As shown in **Exhibit II-9** , we’ll provide the 79 maintenance staff required in Exhibit E. Minimum Staffing Plan. **We’ve added a key role to support our goal to optimize PM measures vs. CM needs: John Loucks-Powell as Director of Asset Management.** John will draw from his experience as a utility owner to direct teams’ day-to-day execution, aiming to exceed the 85% PM/15% CM target.

###### 3. PM STAFFING AND SCHEDULE DISCIPLINE 

We’ll assign PM staff based on the asset-criticality scale we develop together, including the skill sets required for each task. Supervisors will be accountable for weekly completion and documentation standards in the CMMS. Our planners will manage a rolling 4- to 6-week planning cycle that locks in craft hours, access windows, and outage coordination—ensuring high-priority PMs are completed on time without disrupting critical corrective work. 

To keep crews ready and productive, procurement and warehouse staff will participate in weekly planning, pre-kit jobs, and manage inventory aligned with the schedule. Pairing planning with kitting ensures maintenance teams are never stalled waiting for parts or materials, an essential factor in consistently meeting PM schedules. 

###### 4. CM STAFFING—RAPID RESPONSE WITHOUT DIMINISHING PM 

We’ll classify and manage CM under the contract’s Level A/B model. Closeout will capture failure mode, root cause, corrective action, and steps to avoid recurrence. Supervisors will triage issues and assign work promptly, while the planner/ scheduler will preserve the PM plan by slotting CM into reserved capacity and using the kits/parts pipeline to minimize downtime. 

We’ll prepare for wet weather/emergency coverage and minimize impacts on the overall PM schedule. This includes storm rosters, on-call rotations, and clear activation triggers coordinated with Operations. Details on wet weather/ emergencies will be documented in our Emergency Response Plan, provided in **_Section IV.A.4 Safety & Security_** . 

###### 5. PDM INTEGRATION WILL MAXIMIZE PM VALUE 

We’ll provide experts to work with the onsite maintenance teams to make sure MMSD’s desired Level 3 PdM additive elements are optimized to deliver the maximum PM benefits of these tools. These experts, as illustrated in **Exhibit II-9** , will be fully integrated with the onsite maintenance teams and available to address challenges and support opportunities to elevate and expand these tools as MMSD desires in the future. 

###### D. Use of Subcontractors 



We'll balance self-performance requirements with the goal of finding meaningful opportunities for Milwaukee's local and small businesses to support this contract. Our subcontractor plan aims to build lasting capacity within the community through participation in MMSD’s business capacity development initiatives, joint networking events, and mentor–protégé programs. Through close coordination with MMSD’s procurement team and leveraging our subconsultants PRISM and P3 Development Group, we’ll align strategies, share best practices, and expand opportunities for local suppliers and contractors. **Exhibit II-10** lists local strategic partners who will enhance Jacobs’ services by contributing their expertise and insights into MMSD’s facilities and service area.

###### 4. VALUE-ADD: DIGITAL INNOVATION IN TRAINING – DIGITAL TWIN OPERATIONS 

Jacobs can offer MMSD an optional enhancement through our proprietary digital twin operations platform—a value-added innovation designed to optimize operations. If desired by MMSD, it would provide these key benefits: 

- **Realistic Process Understanding** – Operators gain a clear, visual view of system dynamics and facility behavior under different flow and load conditions. 

- **Optimization and Cost Reduction** – By simulating biological nutrient removal, chemical dosing, and energy consumption, the digital twin identifies efficiency gains and potential cost savings. 

- **Immersive Simulation Training** – Connected to a training HMI interface, our digital tools enable operators to rehearse startup, shutdown, and emergency response scenarios in a fully controlled, risk-free setting. 

###### F. Safe Work Environment 



Jacobs and MMSD share a single goal: that **every employee goes home safe each day** . We’ll maintain a safe, compliant, and health-conscious workplace for all MMSD and Jacobs personnel by embedding safety leadership, accountability, and care into every level of the team organization. Our approach aligns directly with the contractual requirements in §3.02(f) and Exhibit M and reflects Jacobs’ culture of BeyondZero®, where safety is a core value built into planning, training, and daily behavior. Jacobs’ **comprehensive safety and security programs** are provided in **_Section IV.A.4_** of this proposal. 

###### 1. FOSTERING A SAFE WORK ENVIRONMENT 

Our **Security and Safety Manager, Travis Leech, will lead project safety, with support from Safety and Security Coordinators at SSWRF and JIWRF** . Together, they’ll provide site-specific leadership and assure alignment across all MMSD facilities. A designated Safety Champion will support the Safety and Security Coordinators as an advocate to frontline staff, helping to reinforce Jacobs’ safety culture and engagement throughout daily operations. Supervisors and team leads will participate in pre-task briefings, permitting, and job hazard analyses to maintain safe, compliant, and efficient work practices. These collective responsibilities will reinforce safety as a shared accountability across the organization. 

retained staff from your current operator, will complete a site-specific orientation before beginning work. Task-specific training is provided for high-risk activities such as confined-space entry, electrical work, fall protection, and chemical handling. Ongoing refreshers and safety stand-downs maintain awareness and reinforce continuous improvement. Every employee has “Stop Work” authority that empowers them to halt unsafe work immediately. Jacobs recognizes and rewards proactive safety behaviors through regular recognition programs that reinforce engagement and accountability. 

We track safety data, audit findings, and incidents using a system called IonCity, which provides analytics for trend identification, root-cause analysis, and correctiveaction verification. This transparent system will provide MMSD leadership real-time visibility into safety performance and outcomes. All activities will comply with OSHA and MMSD requirements, including the procedures outlined in Exhibit M. 

###### 2. HEALTHY WORKFORCE AND WELL-BEING 

Jacobs recognizes that physical safety and mental well-being are inseparable. Our Healthy Employee Focus promotes a holistic approach to care, addressing mental health, wellness, and work-life balance. Employees and their families have access to confidential counseling and mental health resources through Jacobs’ One Million Lives initiative and EAP, along with comprehensive medical and wellness benefits that encourage healthy living. Jacobs also invests in programs that recognize and support employees beyond the workplace, including wellness challenges, family events, and volunteer opportunities that strengthen community connection. These initiatives create a work environment where people feel valued, supported, and motivated to perform at their best.

###### 2. MAINTAINING A RESPECTFUL WORKPLACE OF INCLUSION AND BELONGING 

Inclusion is about tangible action that drives meaningful, measurable change, both in our company and in the communities we serve. It means creating a workplace where our people are curious, embrace different perspectives, and harness new ideas to bring innovative solutions our clients expect. **Exhibit II-14** describes our peer-to-peer culture coach program, designed to make employees feel valued and included.

###### 5. A CULTURE THAT CELEBRATES TEAMWORK AND ACHIEVEMENT 

We recognize what matters: safety, innovation, teamwork, and community service. **Teamwork and empowerment are at the heart of Jacobs’ culture and will power results at MMSD.** We strive to pursue industry recognition that reflects our teams’ operational excellence and client satisfaction. These recognitions reinforce the value of collaboration across functions and levels. 

**As noted in** **_Section II – F,_ safety is the foundation of Jacobs’ operations, and BeyondZero®** anchors health, safety, and well-being for employees and communities. **Through Beyond If℠** , **we celebrate ideas that deliver measurable client impact.** We encourage employees to challenge the status quo and 

develop transformative solutions for global challenges such as climate change and urbanization. Recognition programs highlight individuals and teams whose innovative ideas deliver measurable impact for clients and communities. 

**Jacobs’ commitment to social impact.** Through our **Collectively℠ program** , Jacobs empowers employees to give back via volunteering and charitable contributions. The company matches donations, rewards volunteer hours, and promotes campaigns aligned with UN Sustainable Development Goals. Since 2020, Jacobs employees have contributed over $10.9M to global causes. Each year our teams donate thousands of hours to benefit the local community, from river cleanups and community beautification efforts to charitable golf tournaments and holiday gift and meal drives. **At MMSD, we’ll organize and sustain these efforts with the same discipline we bring to operations, turning community service into a standing practice that provides tangible benefits to Milwaukee, rather than a one-off event.** 

Jacobs’ community pride in Milwaukee is reflected in the number of community-based activities and volunteer programs we’ve participated in over the last 50 years. **_Section 4 – Regional Partnership captures some recent activities_** . 

###### H. Workforce Development 



As your operator, Jacobs will support and strengthen MMSD’s workforce development and capacity building program and bring new strategies for you to consider. 

###### 1. ELEMENTS OF THE PLAN AND IMPLEMENTATION STRATEGIES 

Our plan to execute the workforce development requirements cited in the scope of services includes these goals: 

- Support MMSD’s vision for regional equity and enhanced community benefits. 

- Align with MMSD’s foundation of operational excellence, regulatory reliability, and long-term employee growth.

**The following section and Exhibit II-17** present a workforce development plan designed to meet MMSD requirements. We’ll partner with MMSD and our subcontractors PRISM and P3 Development Group to implement this plan. 

###### STRUCTURED CAREEEXHIBIT II-18. STRUCTU **R** ED CAREER PATHWAYS PATHWAYS 

###### ~~EXHIBIT II-17.~~ 

By integrating formal education, on-the-job experience, and performance-based incentives, this career pathway creates a transparent, motivating structure that benefits both employees and MMSD, which delivers operational resilience and long-term success. 

###### **1.1. Structured Career Pathways Promote Advancement Opportunities** 

A structured pathway makes sure that every employee has a clear roadmap for growth, supported by targeted training and measurable milestones. It strengthens retention, builds institutional knowledge, and creates a future-ready workforce capable of meeting evolving regulatory and technological demands. 

As part of our training plans, we’ll offer structured career pathways that begin with entry-level roles and progress through licensed operator positions, specialized maintenance roles, and supervisory leadership positions. Our focus will be on attracting new employees and maintaining and expanding the skillset of the existing staff. We’ll leverage our existing partnerships with local colleges, high schools, and other regional career-building programs, such as MATC, University of Wisconsin– Milwaukee (UWM), and Marquette. As part of our goals for employee advancement, we’ll specify measurable promotion criteria, defined competencies, and certification milestones, all under the guidance and supervision of a seasoned manager and/or mentor. **Exhibit II-18** illustrates the “ladder” of advancement available to an entrylevel employee interested in becoming a wastewater operator.

###### **1.4. Bolstering Career Awareness** 

###### **1.3. Internships Peak Interest in Careers** 

In support of MMSD’s goals for inclusive growth and technical advancement, we’ll work with MMSD to reinforce its brand and raise the awareness of job and career opportunities, particularly for under-represented communities through targeted outreach activities. We’ll continue to invest in local partnerships to help build the next generation of water professionals to carry MMSD’s legacy forward. This plan builds on the many outreach programs that MMSD and Jacobs have developed or have supported in the region, such as _Camp RISE, Business Capacity Development Program, Waves of Change, the Water & Sewer School, Wisconsin Rural Water Association_ and _Fresh Coast Ambassador Program_ . 

Internships are critical to our recruitment strategies and have been a highly effective component of our operating model in Milwaukee and the surrounding geographic area, where we’ve engaged local colleges, technical associations, and nonprofit organizations such as the UWM and MATC, to attract students and provide insights into career pathways. We’ll continue to work with the City and MMSD as a participant in Camp RISE internship programs, and will review other proposed activities with MMSD, including participation in regional workforce programs, hosting career fairs and plant tours, developing bilingual recruitment tools, and supporting targeted outreach to underserved communities. Jacobs will provide technical instruction, facility tours, and mentoring opportunities that expose Milwaukee youth to hands-on STEM and sustainability careers. 

###### EXHIBIT II-20. OUR COMMITMENT TO UW–MILWAUKEE 

One successful program we propose to replicate under this MMSD contract is a **Wastewater Career Internship Pipeline Program** , which we designed to create a direct pathway for high school graduates to enter apprenticeships or entry-level positions in wastewater operations and maintenance activities, laboratory services, and green infrastructure (groundskeeping and BMP maintenance). The program transitions into paid internships that can lead to long-term careers in the water sector. The program consists of two phases, as described in **Exhibit II-19** . **Exhibit II-20** provides details on our planned partnership with UWM.

###### 2. DEDICATED WORKFORCE DEVELOPMENT TEAM AND REPORTING 

Rick Warner, Director of Regional Partnerships, will direct workforce development planning. Toyin Ogunfolaju will report to Rick and manage workforce development program planning and implementation. Our workforce development team will collaborate with our HR and operations leadership team and MMSD to align career development initiatives with staffing needs, and to assure that internship and other entry-level trainees are onboarded in accordance with our hiring guidelines. 

- PRISM will provide workforce development coordination and support our team in program planning, partnerships, tracking, and reporting. 

- P3 will support our career awareness outreach efforts and capacity building activities. 

We’ll provide MMSD with transparent reporting regarding workforce development that defines and tracks measurable outcomes. We’ll submit a quarterly report/review to the governance committees and an annual report to MMSD to maximize transparency, compliance, and accountability. The Workforce Development Coordinator will submit updates to the annual Workforce Development Plan by January 31 of each contract year and an annual report by March 31 of each contract year that details workforce development activities from the previous contract. **Exhibit II-21** provides examples of our outreach and mentoring strategies to help small businesses develop. 

**Jacobs supports the growth of emerging small businesses through creative outreach and mentoring. Small businesses represent on average 30-35% of our total supply chain spend.**

III 

## PRICING 

This narrative outlines the basis for our pricing, including key assumptions and areas where proposers’ alignment may be needed to ensure an equitable, applesto-apples comparison. MMSD’s contract structure is intentionally prescriptive: the Base Fee funds all minimum staffing, preventive maintenance (PM), safety, security, documentation, and performance standards. Under this model, accurate and responsible pricing is essential to sustaining operational reliability. 

Jacobs has priced this contract as an operating commitment, with the funding necessary to sustain reliable operations and maintain a stable, high-performing workforce. Experience across large municipal O&M contracts shows that underfunded pricing could lead to deferred PM, increased corrective maintenance (CM), staffing instability, and reactive management. Our pricing is intentionally structured to prevent these risks. 

###### A. Staffing and Labor Cost Basis 



###### **Above and Beyond Minimum Staffing** 

Jacobs’ Base Fee fully incorporates all positions required under RFP Exhibit E – Minimum Staffing Plan and RFP Exhibit B section DD.c – Internship and Co-op Program. In addition, we’ve included several “Above and Beyond” roles within the Base Fee to improve service quality, mitigate operational risks, and ensure compliance. Excluding regional and national support, this represents 9.5 additional FTEs beyond the minimum requirements. **_Section II. Staffing, Exhibit II-5_** on page II-5 provides further details on our Staffing Plan. 

Jacobs’ proposal offers stabilized staffing at or above the Minimum Staffing Plan to address the chronic vacancies facing MMSD. Our approach does not rely on deferred work, informal role stacking, or unplanned overtime. Each position is resourced to deliver the defined scope, ensuring stable operations, predictable performance, and a sustainable workforce model from Day 1. 

###### **Preventive, Corrective, and Task Order Labor** 

- PM labor is fully included in the Base Fee and was developed bottom-up using asset counts, PM schedules, and job planning expectations described in **_Section IV.A.3 Maintenance Approach_** . 

- CM labor was estimated at approximately 57,000 hours annually (~32 FTEs), consistent with 2023–2024 CMMS history. These hours are not included in the 



Base Fee and are priced in accordance with the CM fee structure. Given deferred maintenance concerns, we believe some “catchup work” may initially be required. 

- Approximately 4.5 FTEs are allocated to task order completion (sewer cleaning, CCTV work, etc.). Sewer cleaning and televising on pipes with a diameter greater than 48” will be subcontracted, consistent with current practice. Accordingly, these are not included in the Base Fee and are separate from the 32 FTEs shown for CM. 

###### **Wage Rates and Overtime** 

- Represented labor wage rates are adjusted for cost-of-living increases based on current collective bargaining agreement rates. Non-represented and supervisory wage rates were informed by market benchmarking, comparable contracts, and Jacobs’ actual cost experience to ensure we can attract and retain quality staff. 

- Overtime required to meet Base Fee and PM obligations is included in the Base Fee. Overtime associated with CM activities is assumed to be reimbursed at applicable rates, when approved by MMSD. 

###### **Electricity and Onsite Generation** 

Jacobs’ pricing reflects a clear expectation of improved power generation system reliability relative to 2024 actuals, enabled by disciplined asset management and rigorous maintenance execution. 

- At JIWRF, our estimate assumes an average annual turbine output of approximately 9 MW, a level achieved as recently as 2022 but materially higher than 2024 performance. 

- At SSWRF, pricing reflects improved cogeneration availability informed by historical trends and targeted reliability practices. 

These assumptions are supported by PM and reliability management practices described elsewhere in our proposal. MMSD retains full visibility into system performance through existing governance, reporting, review/approval of applicable CM work, and audit mechanisms. 

###### B. Natural Gas 



Natural gas quantities were modeled by end use, including turbine fuel, dryer fuel demand, and balance-of-plant consumption. Dryer gas assumptions reflect heat recovery credits associated with turbine operation.

III. PRICING 

###### C. Chemicals and Process Consumables 



- Chemical quantities were developed from historical actual consumption. 

- SSWRF pricing assumes continued ferric chloride addition and RAS chlorination consistent with 2024 operations until aeration upgrades materially change treatment dynamics. JIWRF pricing assumes continued dual conditioning with ferric and polymer to maintain Milorganite product specifications. 

- Historically, SSWRF has added both _ferric_ chloride and _ferrous_ chloride to the primary clarifiers; ferrous being used as a lower cost ferric alternative.  We’ve normalized historic chemical flows around total Iron (Fe) added and assumed ferric as the sole coagulant for CEPT - this is a $300,000 per year premium over historic practices (after the 60/40 split). 

- Our proposal also includes provision for a chemically enhanced primary treatment (CEPT) polymer companion, guided by our SMEs. This provision adds ~$400,000 to our chemicals price after the 60/40 cost sharing split. 

- We expect to re-evaluate both iron salt and CEPT polymer addition and the return activated sludge chlorination practices after completion of the ongoing aeration system upgrade. 

- Unit pricing reflects the favorable pricing we secure through our strategic sourcing group, with quantities reflecting anticipated reductions achieved through intelligent O&M optimization and our investment in dual-feed coagulant functionality at SSWRF. These efficiencies are incorporated while fully maintaining regulatory and product compliance. 

###### D. Maintenance Economics and Cost Stability 



In asset-intensive O&M contracts, underfunding PM and supporting functions quickly leads to increased CM volume, added administrative burden, and increased governance friction. Jacobs’ Base Fee is intentionally structured to fund PM and its enabling functions at a level required to sustain performance and avoid cost migration into CM and administrative rework. Our pricing reflects the reality that PM, planning, documentation, and material readiness are not overhead. Rather, they are the mechanisms that allow MMSD’s performance standards to be met consistently over the life of the contract. 

###### E. Other Base Fee and Contractual Cost Elements 



Non-WRF utilities (potable water and fuel oil) were estimated based on historical actuals. All remaining Base Fee cost categories are fully included, including solid waste, insurance, fleet maintenance, PPE, security subcontractor support, janitorial services, permit fees, laboratory costs, IT support, and general operating supplies. No material Base Fee scope elements have been excluded or deferred. 

Markup percentages for allowable reimbursable costs, inclusive of overhead and profit components are provided in the Price Proposal Worksheet in accordance with the RFP. 

Our financial proposition to MMSD anticipates payment for the pre-term costs, as reflected in the associated price form. 

Pricing assumptions do not embed contingency for Differing Conditions, which are addressed through the contractual adjustment mechanism. 

###### F. Potential Saving Opportunities 



From a pricing perspective, there are several areas that could offer meaningful cost-saving opportunities for MMSD, should MMSD wish to pursue them. Examples include: 

- **Insurance requirements –** Aligning the insurance language with Jacobs’ corporate policy **would reduce the annual cost by approximately $1,143,500** 

- **Inflationary adjustment index –** Amending the index to reflect our proposed modification **would generate an additional $682,000 in annual savings** 

- **Staffing levels –** Our price includes **9.5 Above‑and‑Beyond FTEs** . These positions can be scaled **if MMSD prefers a lower‑cost option (up to $1,300,000/year)** 

Jacobs remains fully flexible and open to discussing these items—or any other mechanisms MMSD may wish to consider—to arrive at a pricing structure that best supports MMSD’s priorities. 

###### G. Closing Statement 



Our price reflects Jacobs’ commitment to deliver compliant, reliable, and transparent O&M services from Day 1, while strengthening system performance, workforce stability, and long-term value to MMSD. Our pricing is structured to succeed operationally within MMSD’s governance and risk framework and includes targeted investments that enhance performance, reduce risk, and align with industry best practices. In addition, Section IV outlines future improvement opportunities that further optimize costs and increase value, reinforcing our shared commitment to continuous improvement and measurable progress toward the 2035 Vision. 

**Together, MMSD and Jacobs can build a future defined by reliable operations, disciplined maintenance, transparent performance, and measurable community value. Our approach strengthens your workforce, elevates system resilience, advances innovation, and delivers sustainable cost control. With a seamless transition, proven O&M leadership, and a partnership grounded in accountability and shared purpose, we are ready to help MMSD accelerate performance today while positioning MMSD for long-term success.**

###### WE UNDERSTAND MMSD’S FACILITIES AND GOALS 

###### SHARED VALUES, STRONGER OUTCOMES: A COLLABORATIVE APPROACH ALIGNED WITH MMSD’S VALUES FOR RESILIENCE AND EQUITY 

Over the past 2 years, our team, including subject matter experts (SMEs) in O&M, wastewater and solids treatment, conveyance, asset management, system optimization, safety, and security has met with MMSD staff, evaluated your facilities, and carefully reviewed all data provided. Our priorities align with MMSD’s, and our operating approach is built to deliver safe, compliant, and reliable service. With strong emphasis on maintenance, we’ll maximize the lifespan of MMSD’s assets while optimizing long-term costs. **Exhibit IV-4** provides a highlevel assessment of MMSD’s facilities, summary of key findings, and our proposed value-added solutions to strengthen overall system performance. 

Aligning our approach with shared values ensures that every step of our partnership reflects not just what we do, but who we are, together. With the One Team, One Water, One Vision approach illustrated in **Exhibit IV-3,** we align with your strategic objectives below: 

- Commitment to Cost Effective and Compliant Operations and Management of MMSD Facilities 

- Proactively Manage Assets to Extend the Life of MMSD’s Facilities 

**We understand your operations, environmental stewardship priorities, regulatory mandates, and long-term vision. Through discussions and more than 16,000 hours of due diligence, we assessed how best to meet your needs. Drawing on both regional and national expertise, our tailored approach delivers innovative, cost-effective operational strategies that enhance performance and align with your goals.** 

- Stay Ahead of Regulatory Pressures and Emerging Contaminants 

- Adopt Climate Resilient and Energy Efficient Strategies 

- Leadership in Water Quality Innovation through Engagement of Industry Leaders 

- Integrated Watershed Management

###### 1. APPROACH TO MANAGEMENT OF FACILITIES 

Sustaining strong, long-term partnerships is at the heart of our client-for-life mindset. Effective management requires a dynamic approach—one that evolves alongside changing needs and goals. We leverage the full breadth of our integrated resources to deliver the high-quality, cost-effective performance that clients like MMSD expect. We are deeply committed to our clients, our staff, and the communities we serve. By building trust and staying attuned to the evolving priorities of each stakeholder, we maintain alignment that drives shared success. 

###### **1.1. Key Focus Areas in Delivering Excellence** 

Our approach to delivering exceptional results is anchored in five tenets that define how we operate, collaborate, and grow. The tenets, shown in **Exhibit IV-5** create a holistic framework that powers excellence, lasting partnerships, and meaningful impacts. 

###### **1.2. PgM Framework is the Foundation of Our Shared Success** 

**Delivering the scope of services for MMSD—spanning O&M of multiple large facilities, large-scale capital improvement coordination and support, system optimization, regulatory compliance, and long-term environmental stewardship—demands a strategy that goes beyond the current approach.** MMSD’s portfolio is among the most complex and interconnected in the country. Decisions in one area influence operations, customer service, maintenance, and community outcomes across the entire service territory. 

**To meet this level of complexity, innovative and integrated PgM is essential.** Our methodology is built for environments like MMSD’s, where success depends on aligning diverse stakeholders, managing interdependent workstreams, and maintaining transparency across broad and evolving priorities. By applying a program-wide lens, we create the structure, governance, and collaborative culture needed to deliver consistent, predictable, and high value outcomes that support our shared mission of protecting public health and the environment. 

Our PgM approach is built on collaboration, transparency, and accountability and forms the foundation for true stakeholder alignment, as illustrated in **Exhibit IV-6** . This framework integrates proven principles, platforms, procedures, processes, and tools—each refined through a comprehensive readiness assessment during the pre-term contract phase, including team chartering with MMSD and Jacobs’ leadership. The chartering process establishes a clear project vision, goals, and objectives, which shape the governance model—the central structure that drives a collaborative program organization and a transparent culture. 

By viewing contract execution through a PgM lens, we strengthen collaboration and eliminate common disconnects across capital development, customer service, operations, and maintenance. Together, this approach ensures alignment, reinforces a culture of openness, and delivers successful outcomes for all stakeholders.

IV. APPROACH 



###### **1.2.1. How We’ll Successfully Stand Up our PgM Framework for MMSD** 

###### ABOVE AND BEYOND VALUE-ADDED EXTRAS 

We’ll follow the seven-step PgM initiation process summarized in **Exhibit IV-8** to develop a strategy, budget baselines, forecasts, implementation plans, a digital platform, and training for the entire MMSD organization. 

**Keoki Sears** will lead our Program Management Framework for MMSD. Keoki is Jacobs’ global director of program management services and brings 35 years of experience leading programs around the world. 



###### **1.2.2. PgM-Driven Cost Control, Monitoring, Communications, and Reporting Tools for Transparency and Accountability** 

Jacobs’ PgM framework will be the backbone for how we **monitor, control, and communicate operational performance and costs** across MMSD’s operations. In partnership with MMSD, we’ll **align operational monitoring to RFP Exhibit C Standards of Performance** , jointly define key performance indicators (KPIs), and use integrated data from NEXTGEN, SCADA, CMMS, Hach WIMS/LIMS, and financial systems to produce **one validated, single source of truth.** We’ll provide MMSD with real-time dashboards, automated exception tracking, and structured monthly/quarterly reviews that will drive corrective actions and continuous improvement. 



**Quitterie Cotten** , who offers 28 years of wastewater experience, will help Keoki implement this program. See **_Section II – Statement on Staffing, Training, and Corporate Culture_** for more information on our Program Management team structure. 



A critical PgM outcome is **shared-cost transparency and reporting** . Our framework establishes **complete, auditable shared-cost controls** for energy, chemicals, fuel, and biosolids handling—linking consumption and performance drivers directly to invoicing, inventory, and operational conditions. The system will **automatically flag KPI deviations** , maintain a **shared corrective-action log visible to MMSD** , and trigger **immediate notifications for any condition that could impact permit compliance** , followed by written corrective summaries. PgM-led reporting will include **daily operations logs** , **monthly performance and reconciled shared-cost reports** , **quarterly and annual committee-ready summaries** , and an **Annual Performance and Optimization Report.** 



###### EXHIBIT IV-7. ESTABLISHING LEADERSHIP ALIGNMENT 

Team dynamics are critical to performance. Insights Discovery provides a framework to build trust, strengthen collaboration, and create a shared language for alignment. We launched our PgM Framework by assessing the leadership team and plan to extend the assessment to MMSD staff. Our goal is to build a positive, aligned culture that enables MMSD and Jacobs operate as a unified partnership.

**These reports are** generated from validated datasets and accessible to MMSD through secure dashboards to support collaborative oversight, defensible cost allocation, and sustained performance improvement. **_Section 2.6 Monitoring, Tracking, and Reporting Operational Parameters_** provides a detailed discussion of our approach to delivering these tools. 

We’ll work hard to foster day-to-day communication between MMSD and Jacobs staff to empower decision making on all levels and ensure immediate attention and response. Project Manager Kevin Dahl and the entire team will promote the flow of information and transparency. In addition to frequent informal daily communications, our PgM approach will include formal weekly, monthly, and quarterly meetings to review plant performance and maintenance activities in addition to the formal Governance Committees' meetings. ###### **2.1. Collaborative Decision-Making for Safe, Compliant, and Reliable Operations** 

**Collaborative decision-making for safe,  compliant, and reliable operations** 

Jacobs uses system-wide data analytics and digital tools ( **_discussed in Section 2.3_** ) to support decisions. Whether related to process control, odor, energy and chemical use, Milorganite production, staffing, or maintenance, we’ll make decisions with accountability and transparency. 

> **and reliable** Kevin’s management and decision approach is summarized in **Exhibit IV-12** . Experienced, certified staff under the Project Manager’s direction 

> **operations** will make day-to-day O&M decisions using real-time SCADA, laboratory, and instrumentation data aligned with SOPs and permit requirements. Continuous monitoring, predictive maintenance (PdM) insights, and open team communication ensure timely, well-informed decisions and 

> 372_007CAM_1 escalation to MMSD when broader impacts are identified. We’ll address routine matters collaboratively through the O&M Committees, where Jacobs and MMSD will review performance, trends, risks, and upcoming needs and take prompt action without unnecessary escalation. We’ll elevate decisions with cost, policy, or contractual implications to the Executive Committee in accordance with RFP Exhibit P requirements, so MMSD retains full oversight. This disciplined governance approach ensures decisions are made at the right level, by the right people, and at the right time—balancing operational agility with accountability and control. 

In areas of shared risk such as energy, chemicals, odor control, solids handling and disposal, technology and IT systems, and regulatory fees, Jacobs will take a proactive decision approach with no surprises. We’ll communicate issues and decisions early through the governance forums using clearly defined decision protocols, decision logs, and escalation paths to ensure accountability, traceability, and transparency. 

Our decision-making hierarchy presented in **Exhibit IV-13** integrates three complementary tiers, aligned with Exhibit P governance roles and authorities.

###### EXHIBIT IV-13. DECISION-MAKING HIERARCHY 

###### **01 ONSITE PROCESS CONTROL AND OPERATIONAL AND MAINTENANCE DECISIONS** 

Day to day process adjustments or maintenance decisions made by operators under the direction of Project Manager and onsite leadership **Decision Forum:** Daily or Weekly Meetings 

This includes decisions guided by real-time data from SCADA, laboratory, and instrumentation systems. They are verified against established SOPs and compliance requirements such as the governing permits and the stipulated standards of performance. Key operating parameters including aeration, flow distribution, chemical dosing, and energy use are continuously monitored through dashboards, trend analysis, and performance alerts to support timely, defensible decisions. 

Maintenance decisions impacting operations are guided by PM and PdM data, complemented by real-time insights from our team monitoring equipment daily. This approach helps identify factors that could compromise data accuracy, such as faulty or uncalibrated instruments. All staff are empowered to share observations during weekly meetings or as needed, enabling timely decisions or escalation to MMSD when broader implications arise. 

###### **02 COLLABORATIVE OVERSIGHT AND OPTIMIZATION** 

Operational decisions with broader implications evaluated collaboratively through regular meetings and discussions with subject matter experts and MMSD 

**Decision Forum:** Operations and Maintenance Committees 

###### **03 STRATEGIC AND CONTINUOUS IMPROVEMENT** 

Longer term or cross functional decisions evaluated by Jacobs SMEs and analytics specialist, discussed and reviewed with MMSD **Decision Forum:** Executive Committee 

This includes energy management strategies, wet weather response, tunnel operations, and Milorganite production. These decisions are data-driven, have shared accountability, focus on system improvements, and ensure alignment with MMSD’s financial, environmental, and reliability goals. 

Operational and maintenance performance metrics are reviewed monthly with MMSD to confirm alignment, document decision rationale, and evaluate outcomes. Jacobs’ operations, maintenance, and technical leadership will work directly with MMSD, ensuring coordinated decision-making and consistent application of agreed strategies. 

This includes decisions such as process optimization, resource recovery initiatives, or technology implementation. SMEs review data trends, conduct root cause and cost-benefit analyses, and provide recommendations that are formally presented through the Annual Innovation Workshop and, as appropriate, the Executive Committee defined in Exhibit P. 

Strategic recommendations are grounded in predictive analytics, regulatory outlooks, and life-cycle cost assessments to ensure sustainable, long-term value for MMSD. They ensure operational and investment decisions reflect MMSD’s commitment to stewardship of public funds.

###### IV. APPROACH 

###### SECTION 2.2 

**2.2. Effective Process Monitoring and Control for Effective process System-Wide Insight and Operational Excellence monitoring and control for system-** MMSD has one of the nation’s most complex wastewater systems, **wide insight** including a 4,800-mile collection network, numerous pump stations, **for  operational** interplant sludge pumping, an ISS for wet weather flow, the SSWRF, **excellence** and JIWRF with significant Milorganite fertilizer production. These 373_007CAM_1 assets function as a single, interconnected system, and operational decisions in one area directly influence performance, reliability, and compliance elsewhere. Achieving operational excellence requires system-wide insight into how each element interacts with another. To translate this into actionable strategies and to enable operators to make informed and timely decisions, our approach focuses on the following: 

- **Integrated Cross-Facility Monitoring and Best-Practice Control:** We’ll combine SCADA data, online sensors, laboratory results, process models, and operator observations into a single unified view that enables early detection of upsets, rapid troubleshooting, and precise control of aeration, clarifiers, chemical dosing, sludge handling, and solids processing. Because SSWRF and JIWRF are operationally linked through the ISS, solids transfers, and system hydraulics, we’ll emphasize coordinated decision-making across facilities. Operators at both WRFs will work from shared dashboards, common data sets, and aligned control strategies to protect compliance while optimizing energy use, hydraulics management, and solids management. 

- **Predictive Operating Insight:** To anticipate operating conditions before they develop, we pair operator knowledge with advanced digital tools—such as Intelligent O&M analytics, process models, and a digital twin—allowing staff to prepare for changes in loading, tunnel discharges, equipment stress, or solids characteristics and maintaining stability across the system. Details on these digital tools are included in **_Section 2.3_** . 

- **Optimization of Energy, Chemicals, Solids, and Process Stability** : Adjustments to aeration strategies, chemical addition, wasting rates, solids loading, and dryer feed management have direct impacts on energy consumption, biosolids quality, wastewater treatment, odor, and overall system resilience. Jacobs’ O&M and technical teams will continuously evaluate and optimize these areas while preserving stable, compliant operations. 

We’ve identified **key operational focus areas in Exhibit IV-14** that offer the biggest opportunities to improve performance across MMSD’s system. Each requires specialized expertise and proven digital tools for success. We introduce each key focus area in this exhibit, then describe our approach for each in more detail.

###### KEY OPERATIONAL FOCUS AREAS EXHIBIT IV-14. KEY OPERATIONAL FOCUS AREAS 



###### **COLLECTION SYSTEM &  WET-WEATHER FLOW MANAGEMENT** 

We will apply predictive forecasting, coordinated tunnel and WRF operations, and disciplined decision-making to support MMSD’s goals of zero overflows, reduced basement backups, and optimized energy use. Our approach emphasizes proactive readiness, resilient wetweather response, and close coordination with MMSD management. 



###### **LIQUID TREATMENT PROCESS CONTROL** 

Using disciplined, data-driven process control, we will integrate primary, secondary, and disinfection processes to optimize biological performance, aeration, solids management, energy use, and effluent quality—ensuring stable operations and continuous compliance. 



###### **SOLIDS PROCESSING & MILORGANITE  PRODUCTION** 

Building on MMSD’s strong performance, we will optimize sludge handling, energy recovery, and product quality across both WRFs. Our approach enhances reliability, maximizes renewable energy use, and ensures consistent Milorganite production through targeted optimization and disciplined risk management. 



###### **ENERGY OPTIMIZATION** 

We will operate power generation, heat recovery, and high-energy treatment processes as a single integrated system. Through predictive analytics and systemwide energy modeling, we will reduce energy costs and demand charges while supporting MMSD’s 2035 Vision for increased renewable energy without compromising treatment performance. 



###### **CHEMICAL  OPTIMIZATION** 

Guided by regulatory compliance, transparency, cost management, and innovation, Jacobs uses data science and historical best-practice analysis to deliver defensible reductions in chemical use while maintaining stable operations, full compliance, and clear operator visibility into dosing decisions. 



###### **ODOR  CONTROL** 

Our proactive, predictive, and community-focused approach emphasizes odor prevention, rapid identification of potential odor issues, and disciplined response. By combining advanced H2S modeling, real-time monitoring, and transparent communication, we reduce odor incidents and strengthen public trust.

###### _Intelligent Dry Weather Management_ 

We’ll manage dry-weather operations with proactive maintenance, close monitoring, and predictive tools to keep the MIS and ISS ready. Using SCADA data from 250+ real-time flow meters and level sensors, we’ll find I/I trends and work with MMSD to evaluate future use of AquaDNA (a digital tool for anomaly detection) and PdM. **Exhibit IV-16** shows an example of wet weather and dry weather alerts used at Wilmington with AquaDNA. Over time, integrating CMMS, SCADA, IoT sensors, and analytics will give operators a clearer, real-time picture of system capacity and asset health. We’ll operate all pump stations for reliability and efficiency using structured preventative maintenance (PM), vibration and electrical testing, wet well maintenance, and targeted improvements such as deragger sensors at high-ragging sites. Our AquaDNA deragging technology can clear blockages based on monitoring amp draws and keep pumps online before a fault occurs that requires hands on repair. 

been a national leader in wet-weather management, starting with the ISS and its pioneering use of deep tunnel storage. With nearly 30 entry points from the MIS into the ISS, operating the ISS before, during, and after a storm event is both complex and mission critical. Jacobs understands MMSD’s goals of zero overflows by 2035, reduced basement backups, and lower energy use. Our approach supports those outcomes by strengthening the fundamentals: reviewing and refining SOPs and wet-weather procedures, improving tunnel dewatering protocols, and using rainfall and flow data to optimize decision-making. In dry weather, we’ll focus on readiness through PdM while minimizing unnecessary pumping and resulting energy use. In wet weather, we’ll use rainfall patterns, forecast data, and real-time system conditions to balance available tunnel storage and WRF capacity.
