Jacobs’ industry-leading safety performance ( **Exhibit IV-50** ) is supported by proven systems, comprehensive training, and transparent reporting. Safety360™ is Jacobs’ integrated Safety Management System (SMS) that centralizes safety data, reporting, and performance tracking to support proactive risk prevention and continuous improvement. Through Safety360 and Jacobs’ Learning Management System (LMS), MMSD will have full visibility into training records, incident investigations, trends, and corrective actions. 

Jacobs applies structured hazard identification and risk analysis methods to wastewater treatment, solids handling, chemical management, conveyance, and energy systems, and integrates the results into operating procedures, training, and safety programs to reduce risk and support safe, reliable operations. 

###### **4.1.2. SMS and Required Plans** 

Jacobs’ SMS complies with OSHA 29 CFR 1910 and will be aligned with MMSD procedures. Thirty days prior to the Start Date, we’ll submit a complete Safety Plan that includes: 

- Confined space entry requirements, including permitting, continuous monitoring, communication, and rescue readiness 

- Gas detection practices and calibration, as well as documentation of fixed and portable monitoring equipment 

- Lockout and tagout procedures and verification of isolation 

- Arc-flash protection, safe electrical work practices, and labeling compliance 

- Chemical hygiene requirements and laboratory safety protocols consistent with MMSD standards 

- Housekeeping and combustible dust control in Milorganite drying areas 

- Daily pre-task planning, Activity Hazard Analyses, and safe work permitting 

- Expectations for subcontractor safety, coordination, and oversight 

Jacobs will work in close coordination with MMSD’s Risk Manager and Safety Advisor to ensure consistent implementation, documentation, inspection practices, and reporting.

IV. APPROACH 

###### **4.1.3. Leadership, Roles, and Integration** 

Safety leadership is embedded in our on-site team. The Project Manager will hold overall accountability for safety performance and will serve as the primary point of communication with MMSD’s Risk Manager and Safety Advisor. A dedicated **Safety and Security Manager** will implement the SMS, conduct inspections, coordinate training, and oversee investigations and documentation. Each facility will also have a safety coordinator to reinforce daily safe work practices and encourage peer engagement. Supervisors will lead pre-task briefings, hazard identification, and verification that safety procedures and permits are correctly implemented. 

Jacobs and MMSD will jointly review incidents, corrective actions, and trends during quarterly **Operations Governance Committee** meetings to promote continuous improvement. 

###### **4.1.4. Training and Workforce Engagement** 

Employees assigned to MMSD’s facilities will complete required OSHA and MMSD-specific training, including confined space entry, lockout and tagout, fall protection, respiratory protection, combustible dust awareness, hazard communication, emergency response, chemical hygiene, and first aid and CPR. Job-specific and sitespecific modules will supplement these core requirements. Annual refresher training will be tracked in the LMS and made available to MMSD. 

Regular engagement through daily start-up discussions, weekly toolbox talks, and safety observations will reinforce awareness, support proactive hazard identification, and encourage open communication. All personnel will have stop-work authority. 

###### **4.1.5. Incident Reporting and Investigation** 

Jacobs will meet MMSD’s notification and reporting procedures for all safety incidents, near misses, injuries, or hazardous conditions. MMSD’s Risk Manager, Safety Advisor, and Operations Officer will be notified immediately by phone. We’ll provide a written summary within 12 hours, followed by a full investigation, including root cause analysis and corrective actions, within the required 48 to 72-hour period. All findings will be managed through Safety360 and reviewed with MMSD during routine performance discussions.

###### **4.2. Site Physical Security** 

###### **4.2.1. Security, Integration, Prevention, and Rapid Response** 

Jacobs’ Global Security and Resilience team, supported by specialized technical experts, brings more than 100 years of combined operational and technical experience securing water, wastewater, and other highly sensitive government facilities across the United States. Jacobs is widely recognized as a leader in safety and security for the water sector, with experts who **helped develop federal security guidance for water and wastewater treatment facilities in collaboration with the U.S. Environmental Protection Agency, the Water Research Foundation, and the Department of Homeland Security** . These standards continue to serve as the industry benchmark for protecting critical infrastructure, and MMSD will benefit directly from the same proven practices that have shaped national policy. 

Jacobs will deliver a fully integrated Safety and Security Program that protects people, assets, and information through a single, coordinated framework. Consistent with the mission-critical designation of MMSD facilities as Department of Homeland Security Critical Infrastructure, our approach emphasizes prevention, real-time monitoring, and rapid response to maintain secure operations across all sites. 

Aligned with RFP Exhibit M requirements, Jacobs’ program integrates physical security and personnel safety into a proactive management system that ensures consistency, transparency, and accountability. Through dedicated staffing, established procedures, advanced digital tools, and close coordination with MMSD’s Risk Management and IT teams, Jacobs will deliver a security program built on prevention, readiness, and continuous improvement. 

###### **4.2.2. Security Management Structure** 

Jacobs’ **Safety and Security Management Program (SSMP)** integrates dedicated personnel, technology systems, and corporate oversight to maintain a safe and secure environment across all MMSD facilities. A **full-time Security and Safety Manager** will report to the Project Manager and lead SSMP implementation across all sites, coordinating directly with MMSD’s Risk Manager to ensure full compliance with Exhibit M requirements. This role will be supported by two site-based positions: a Safety and **Security Coordinator at JIWRF and a Safety and Security Coordinator at SSWRF** . Together, these leaders will manage site safety initiatives and security operations, including access control, guard coverage, patrol scheduling, and daily coordination with plant staff. 

The SSMP structure includes reach-back support from Jacobs’ Corporate Health, Safety, Security, and Environment organization while maintaining direct, ongoing coordination with MMSD’s Risk Manager, Safety Advisor, and Operations Officer. This integrated structure enables continuous improvement, rapid issue resolution, and focused oversight by experienced professionals from both MMSD and Jacobs. Jacobs will also participate in quarterly joint security and risk management meetings with MMSD to review incidents, technology performance, audit results, and emerging risks.

###### IV. APPROACH 

Jacobs will manage MMSD’s access control and surveillance infrastructure, including periodic, client-approved access audits to verify compliance. Surveillance systems at JIWRF, SSWRF, and the Thirteenth Street Garage will be centrally monitored using the Genetec platform at a location mutually determined with MMSD leadership. Jacobs will perform PM, routine functionality checks, and coordinate closely with MMSD IT to ensure timely resolution of outages. Key and lock control will be centrally managed using controlled issuance and inventory tracking to maintain accountability and alignment with MMSD procedures. 

###### **4.2.4. Incident Reporting and Response** 

Jacobs will maintain written, MMSD-approved Site Security Procedures consistent with Exhibit M, defining responsibilities, escalation paths, and required staff training. These procedures will align with MMSD’s timelines for reporting security events including, but not limited to unknown intruder or trespasser, theft, vandalism, drone incursion and facility breaches to ensure a coordinated rapid response and action through MMSD’s Control Room or Process Monitoring Room (PMR); when necessary, relevant law enforcement agency will be contacted along with operations management. 

Jacobs recognizes that MMSD’s security responsibilities extend beyond treatment facilities to include entry gate access control, pump stations, conveyance assets, and remote monitoring locations. To support these requirements, Jacobs can integrate alarms from MMSD’s SCADA systems into the security platform to provide redundant notification of critical events. This approach enables resilient, continuous monitoring of security conditions through the security control center in parallel with MMSD’s Operations Center, providing a dual-layered approach to protection. 

All security and emergency events will follow the same notification hierarchy through MMSD’s PMR to maintain unified coordination across intruder, chemical, fire, or weather-related incidents through a documented security plan. 

###### **4.2.5. Active-Shooter and Workplace-Violence Preparedness** 

In accordance with Exhibit M and MMSD’s Security Procedures, Jacobs will incorporate workplace-violence and active-shooter preparedness into its Safety and Security Plan. All employees will receive awareness training during onboarding and annual refreshers covering recognition of warning signs, communication protocols, and protective actions. Jacobs’ Security and Safety Manager will coordinate onsite response, assist law enforcement, and ensure incident reporting and investigation meet required timelines. Jacobs has a robust active shooter and workplace violence prevention program that is based on recognition of human behaviors that are focused on grievance interruption. The information is based on the FBI publication _"Making Prevention a Reality, Identifying, Assessing and Managing the Threat of Target Attacks.”_ 

###### **4.2.3. Integrated Prevention and Awareness** 

Security awareness is built into every employee’s onboarding and annual training. Jacobs’ BeyondZero® culture reinforces the principle that everyone shares responsibility for maintaining a safe and secure workplace. Staff will be trained to recognize suspicious activity, protect MMSD property and data, and follow established notification protocols. In addition, Jacobs has completed more than 100 Risk and Resilience Assessments using the ANSI J-100 methodology. This expertise enables Jacobs to anticipate, evaluate, and respond effectively to potential threats and vulnerabilities.

###### **4.2.6. Innovative Security Enhancements** 

Jacobs understands that MMSD is implementing important upgrades, including new video cameras, the **Genetec Security Center** platform, and a visitor-enrollment and badging system. These investments establish a strong foundation for an effective, modernized security program. Based on our experience with similar utilities, Jacobs can offer several value-added recommendations that could further strengthen MMSD’s protection and response capabilities. 

###### _Shoreline Intrusion Detection_ 

Jacobs will evaluate opportunities to enhance perimeter protection at JIWRF, including **potential shoreline intrusion-detection solutions** identified through a site-specific vulnerability assessment. Technologies that may be considered include ground-based radar or microwave detection units integrated with existing camera systems. When configured, these systems can automatically cue corresponding camera views in the security office for rapid assessment. 

Such technologies are effective in darkness, fog, snow, and other adverse conditions, and may be supplemented with optional acoustic loudspeakers to issue automated voice warnings and deter trespassers. Jacobs has implemented similar shoreline-protection solutions at waterfront utilities to improve early detection and response to vessels approaching restricted areas and would work collaboratively with MMSD to determine the appropriate application based on site conditions, risk prioritization, and operational preferences. 

###### _Mobile Video Capability_ 

Jacobs recommends enabling Genetec Mobile for MMSD security personnel. This feature allows staff to view live camera feeds and capture video directly from iOS or Android devices, with recordings automatically uploaded to the PMR. Mobile access improves situational awareness for roving officers and strengthens real-time incident documentation. 

###### **4.2.7. Jacobs Business Networks & Infrastructure Cybersecurity Preparedness** 

Jacobs applies a unified cybersecurity approach that integrates automation controls, SCADA, and OT cybersecurity under a single leadership structure. This alignment eliminates silos, strengthens visibility across IT and OT environments, and ensures consistent application of security controls. While protection of MMSD-owned systems is addressed in the Operational Technology Approach section, this section focuses on Jacobs’ enterpriselevel business cybersecurity capabilities that protect corporate networks, data, and collaboration platforms supporting MMSD operations. 

Recognizing the evolving cyber threat landscape facing water and wastewater utilities, Jacobs’ integrated governance framework enhances resilience, accelerates incident response, and provides MMSD confidence that cybersecurity risks are managed consistently across all layers of infrastructure. 

###### _Cybersecurity Framework, Governance, and Incident Response_ 

Jacobs’ cybersecurity program is aligned with ISO 27001 and NIST/CISA standards, providing a globally recognized framework for managing information security risk through documented policies, regular audits, and continuous improvement. Jacobs maintains a comprehensive Cyber Incident Response Plan that defines roles, communication protocols, and restoration procedures, supported by a 24/7 global Security Operations Center for monitoring and rapid containment.

###### **_Enhanced Cybersecurity Controls_** 

- **Advanced Threat Protection:** Jacobs deploys full endpoint detection and response and network detection and response across all business networks and endpoints to identify and stop threats in real time. 

- **Zero-Trust Remote Access:** Secure VPN access is provided through a zero-trust architecture that continuously verifies users and devices before granting network access. 



###### _Automated Security Workflows_ 

Jacobs also recommends configuring Genetec Mission Control to establish predefined security-response workflows. During an incident, the system can guide personnel through required actions such as dispatching guards, notifying law enforcement, alerting nearby staff, locking doors, and activating relevant cameras. Automated workflows support a consistent, timely, and fully auditable response.

###### _Vulnerability Management and Network Protection_ 

Jacobs employs automated patch management and vulnerability scanning to maintain systems within two major vendor releases, with all patches validated for authenticity. Layered defenses include next-generation firewalls, intrusion prevention systems, endpoint detection and response, and continuous network monitoring. Secure remote access is enforced through encrypted VPNs, multi-factor authentication, and zero-trust principles to prevent unauthorized access and lateral movement.

###### _Access Control and Account Management_ 



Jacobs enforces rigorous identity and access management controls based on role-based access, unique user accounts, and least-privilege principles. Account permissions are reviewed periodically and disabled promptly when no longer required. Privilege escalation is controlled through just-in-time access, shared accounts are prohibited, and enhanced credential monitoring is applied to critical and SCADA-adjacent systems. 

###### _Workforce Awareness, Testing, and Threat Detection_ 

Jacobs conducts mandatory annual cybersecurity and critical infrastructure protection training, supplemented by simulated phishing exercises and advanced email filtering. Continuous monitoring using SIEM platforms, behavioral analytics, and automated alerting supports real-time threat detection and proactive threat hunting. Jacobs performs routine penetration testing on its business networks and will coordinate joint testing of protected systems with MMSD, as appropriate. 

###### _Data Protection, Continuous Improvement, and Partnership_ 

Jacobs protects data confidentiality and integrity through AES-256 encryption, automated data classification, version control, and change attribution to support auditability and compliance. Cybersecurity performance will be reviewed quarterly with MMSD’s risk and IT teams to assess metrics, track corrective actions, and incorporate lessons learned from drills, audits, and incidents. 

Jacobs’ 24/7 readiness is supported by Safety360, which analyzes safety and security data across our global portfolio to identify emerging risks and recurring vulnerabilities. Together, Jacobs and MMSD will maintain a culture of vigilance, shared learning, and continuous improvement to protect MMSD’s people, assets, and data. 

###### _Field and Collection System Security_ 

MMSD’s conveyance and collection assets receive the same level of cybersecurity vigilance as treatment facilities. Jacobs will monitor alarms and intrusion alerts from remote sites, conduct access and lighting inspections, and document findings in the CMMS. Integration of CCTV, SCADA, and monitoring systems provides real-time visibility into systemwide security conditions and response actions.

###### 5. CONTINUOUS IMPROVEMENT AND INNOVATION 

Jacobs brings a proven, research-driven approach to continuous improvement through innovation, tailored to MMSD’s evolving challenges. Through collaboration with MMSD and local partners, we’ll co-develop solutions that enhance compliance, resilience, and sustainability. Leveraging our operational expertise and global technology network, we’ll focus on continuous improvements, deliver innovations aligned with MMSD’s 2035 Vision, and ensure knowledge is shared and leveraged across all facets of the contract. Innovation has long been the driving force behind progress in municipal wastewater collection and treatment—making systems more effective, resilient, and sustainable. At Jacobs’, our role as facility operators, consultants, and constructors is deeply rooted in a commitment to innovation through applied research. This hands-on experience has been instrumental in helping our clients design, build, and operate more sustainable and cost-effective wastewater management systems. Our direct involvement in research enables us to develop new knowledge, test and pilot emerging technologies at facilities we operate such as Wilmington and inform policy and decision-making. Our research provides municipalities with the data and insights needed to choose the most effective and sustainable strategies. 

Our approach to continuous improvement and to leveraging innovation during this contract is anchored by several strategic and practical reasons: 

� **Aligning Innovation with MMSD’s Strategic Vision.** MMSD’s 2035 Vision emphasizes sustainability, decarbonization, and resilience. By working together with you, we can ensure that all operational improvements and innovations directly support these long-term goals. This alignment helps prioritize initiatives that deliver the greatest value to MMSD and its stakeholders. 

- **Accelerating Problem-Solving and Adaptation.** MMSD faces evolving challenges, PFAS regulations, climate impacts, aging infrastructure, and workforce transitions. A collaborative innovation model between Jacobs and MMSD allows for rapid identification, piloting, and scaling of solutions tailored to MMSD’s unique context, especially at critical facilities like SSWRF. 

- **Leveraging Complementary Expertise.** MMSD brings deep institutional knowledge and local insight. Jacobs contributes global research, technical depth, and operational experience. Together, this partnership fosters co-creation of solutions that are both technically sound and locally relevant. 

- **Enhancing Transparency and Trust.** Our successful partnerships in other operations contracts highlight the need for a more integrated and communicative relationship. Joint innovation efforts build trust, improve transparency, and ensure that MMSD remains actively engaged in shaping operational strategies. 

- **Ensuring Knowledge Transfer and Capacity Building.** Collaborative innovation supports shared learning, helping MMSD staff stay informed about emerging technologies and best practices, and in discerning how to properly consider them for the benefit of the partnership. It also ensures that innovations are not siloed but embedded into MMSD’s organizational knowledge and future planning. 

###### **5.1. Jacobs Annual Innovation Workshop Drives Continuous Improvements and Elevated O&M** 

**Our Annual Innovation Workshops, provided at no cost to MMSD** , use a proven innovation framework that brings together leading experts to share the most relevant, cutting-edge knowledge in the industry. A clear demonstration of the depth and relevance of our expertise is our unmatched participation in national knowledge sharing events such as the Water Environment Federation Technical Exhibition and Conference (WEFTEC), where Jacobs consistently contributes more technical content and thought leadership than any other O&M provider. For example, Jacobs had 27 papers at the 2024 WEFTEC where our major O&M competitors had none. In 2025, Jacobs presented more than 60 workshops and remained a long-time sponsor of the annual operations challenge, where top wastewater professionals demonstrate their skills in collections, laboratory work, process control, maintenance, and safety. This level of engagement underscores the breadth and relevance of the expertise we can bring. 

Each year, we’ll convene MMSD staff, stakeholders, our onsite O&M team, and Jacobs’ utility experts to review the prior year’s achievements and co-develop new goals and innovations aligned with MMSD’s evolving needs and long-term vision. This forward-looking workshop will help ensure MMSD remains at the forefront of treatment technologies, optimization strategies, and modern O&M practices. 

Jacobs will bring industry recognized leaders in process engineering, biosolids, hydraulics, wet weather flow management, pump station design and operations, digital optimization, and energy management. They are all hand selected to match MMSD’s current challenges and opportunities. Before the workshop, we’ll work closely with MMSD to co-develop a full day agenda that reflects your priorities and expectations, focusing the session on your desired outcomes. Once the agenda is set, our specialists will begin their preparation in advance, learning your facilities, analyzing performance data, and identifying improvement opportunities so they arrive equipped with realistic, actionable ideas tailored specifically to MMSD.

###### **5.3. Value-Added Improvements and Innovations Included as Part of the Base Fee** 

Jacobs is committed to delivering meaningful, measurable improvements from the start of the contract. As part of our Base Fee, we are investing, at **no additional cost to MMSD, a suite of value-added enhancements that directly strengthen day-to-day operations, improve system reliability, and reduce costs.** We developed these offerings through site visits, data review, and discussions with MMSD staff, along with ideas from Jacobs operators, maintenance experts, 

digital specialists, and process SMEs. Together, they reflect our commitment to a performance-driven O&M partnership that will deliver both immediate and longterm operational benefits to MMSD. 

These value-added improvements involve onsite and offsite operational support, application of proven best practices, targeted operational and engineering studies, energy and chemical optimization pilots, solids and Milorganite process enhancements, odor and corrosion modeling, OT modernization and cybersecurity strengthening, and investments that improve system performance and operator effectiveness. **Many of these services—such as Intelligent O&M optimization, WATS modeling, solids-handling improvements, and OT configuration management—are typically delivered through stand-alone consulting engagements. By embedding them directly into our Base Fee, Jacobs provides MMSD with millions of dollars of added value,** accelerates efficiency gains, and gives operators practical tools and expert support that enhance performance at every step of the system.

###### **5.4 Additional Improvements and Innovations for Future Savings** 

In addition to the offerings included in our Base Fee and summarized in **Exhibit IV-52** , Jacobs will partner with MMSD to explore more opportunities to improve and innovate across MMSD’s facilities with the goal of optimizing performance and further reducing shared O&M costs and risks. Our team’s operators and SMEs have brainstormed more than 25 ideas focused on process improvements, intelligent O&M optimizations and security/SCADA/OT improvements that we would like to develop in partnership with MMSD. **These insights are just our initial takeaway from a handful of visits over the past months. Once we're hands-on and embedded with your system, we'll keep coming up with fresh ideas to optimize it.** The ideas presented in **Exhibit IV-53** , have the potential for significant optimization and cost savings. **We believe these ideas can bring MMSD between $21M and $53M in additional value-adds and savings over the next 10 years.**

###### 7. SUSTAINABLE APPROACH TO OPERATIONS 

Jacobs is committed to delivering solutions that make sustainability a core operating discipline—not an aspiration. Under our leadership, sustainable performance becomes measurable, repeatable, and embedded in daily decisionmaking. We’ll establish clear sustainability KPIs such as energy and chemical use per million gallons treated, energy and chemical use per ton of solids processed, MMBtu per ton of Milorganite®, and natural gas MMBtu per ton of Milorganite®, along with greenhouse gas (GHG) intensity and renewable energy contribution. Combining innovation, collaboration, and disciplined execution, we will help MMSD advance its 2035 Vision and position MMSD as a national model for climate-smart, high-performance wastewater operations. 

###### **7.1. Energy Optimization and Decarbonization** 

As discussed earlier, Jacobs has identified significant opportunities for chemical and power optimization at MMSD’s facilities that will **reduce operating costs and greenhouse gas emissions** . Using advanced process modeling, SCADA analytics, and predictive algorithms, Jacobs will optimize aeration, pumping, and solids handling systems. Based on results at comparable large utilities, these measures have achieved **power reductions of 15–25 percent** , with Scope 2 and Scope 3 greenhouse gas reductions occurring proportionally as power and chemical use declines. 

Jacobs will update MMSD’s greenhouse gas inventory and identify priority **emissions-reduction strategies** . Additional Scope 1 and Scope 3 reductions will be evaluated through targeted assessment of resource recovery, fugitive methane, flaring reduction opportunities, process-related N₂O, and other optimization 

measures. Industry-leading tools will be used to quantify emissions and track progress in support of MMSD’s long-term decarbonization objectives. 

Jacobs also brings extensive experience optimizing UV disinfection systems to balance treatment performance, energy demand, and lifecycle cost. As MMSD expands UV use, Jacobs will collaborate with MMSD to manage increased electrical demand through process optimization and alignment with MMSD’s goals for expanding on-site solar generation at SSWRF, supporting progress toward energy self-sufficiency and climate-smart operations. 

###### **7.2. Resource Recovery and Biosolids Optimization** 

Jacobs will enhance MMSD’s longstanding leadership in sustainable resource recovery by **improving the efficiency of biosolids, energy, and reuse operations** . We’ll optimize digester performance and gas production, increase the efficiency of dewatering and drying systems, and reduce fossil fuel demand in Milorganite® production. Working collaboratively with MMSD, we’ll evaluate opportunities to divert high-energy side streams, such as primary scum and high-strength organic waste, into the digesters to boost renewable gas generation and advance **resource recovery** . 

###### **7.3. Green Infrastructure Integration and Climate Resilience.** 

Jacobs will collaborate to support MMSD in meeting its green infrastructure detention capacity goals and adapt to increasing climate variability We will ensure that treatment and conveyance assets continue to operate sustainably and reliably under changing climate conditions protecting public health, safeguarding water resources, and strengthening the region’s long-term resilience.

We recognize that transitioning to a new operator is a significant decision, and it’s natural to have concerns—whether about potential impacts on service levels or the effect on the dedicated staff who keep your facilities running. We’ve addressed these considerations throughout our proposal so MMSD can weigh them against the many benefits that Jacobs’ fresh perspective, proven processes, and commitment to people bring to MMSD and its customers. Jacobs is sensitive to the perception that risk is introduced during a transition, and we dedicate substantial resources to minimize impacts and respond to any challenge that may arise.

###### IV. APPROACH 

**on a wide variety of topics, including compensation and benefits, work-life balance, safety, training, and advancement opportunities** . These areas align with Jacobs’ established employment practices and our long-standing commitment to a safe, supportive, and rewarding work environment. 

Jacobs brings real-world experience and an unmatched track record of success with the most complex and high-profile transitions in the industry. Our transition plan is based on more than 40 years of O&M delivery to leading utilities in the U.S., supported by refined processes and proven tools that allow us to transition projects consistently, confidently, and without disruption. 

Our proven transition model, which focuses on supporting existing employees, has consistently produced exceptional outcomes. **In 2025, four transitions from Veolia resulted in an overwhelming number of staff choosing to join Jacobs, as shown on Exhibit IV-55.** These transitions included the West Basin Municipal Water District, the largest water reuse facility in North America with 58 FTEs, and the City of Jackson’s wastewater system with 39 FTEs, which Veolia highlighted as a reference project during MMSD’s SOQ phase. 

Our 200-point transition plan sequences critical activities-staffing, safety, SOP alignment, SCADA/IT readiness, asset-management integration, and other important activities well in advance of March 1, 2028. For MMSD, this translates into a structured, transparent, and proactive approach that ensures a seamless transfer of responsibility while safeguarding safety, compliance, and workforce stability. 

**Jacobs recognizes that MMSD’s top priorities during transition are uninterrupted operations, full regulatory compliance, and staff continuity** . MMSD’s schedule reflects this commitment by allowing an unprecedented 18-month transition period, far exceeding the industry-standard 90 days. Combined with your procurement schedule and the extended shadowing period, this creates the conditions for an exceptionally smooth and low-risk transition.

The institutional knowledge and technical expertise of MMSD’s current workforce are essential to maintaining safe, reliable, and high-performing operations. Existing employee knowledge is critical to successful Milorganite production. Successful Milorganite production requires precise, coordinated execution across multiple process areas, including blended biosolids management, dewatering, drying, product classification, and silo storage. These activities must be performed safely, consistently, and with unwavering attention to product quality. Jacobs’ transition approach is designed to preserve and leverage this deep operational knowledge while reinforcing best practices, safety systems, and process controls to ensure continuity and long-term success.

###### **1.3.3. Kevin Dahl, CMRT, CRL – Project Manager** 

Kevin has more than 26 years of experience in water and wastewater operations and management. As Project Manager and a core member of the Transition Leadership Team, he’ll work closely with MMSD well before the start date to align staffing, operational strategies, and knowledge transfer with MMSD’s goals. His background spans complex utility operations and operability reviews that enhance efficiency and reduce risk. Kevin led the transition of the City of Wilmington, DE’s 168 MGD wastewater system with (a Veolia transition) and has supported dozens of other major projects across the country. Kevin’s collaborative and transparent leadership will help ensure an organized and effective transition for MMSD. 

###### **1.3.4. Program Management Office (PMO) Role** 

Supporting the Transition Leadership Team, Jacobs’ PMO will provide oversight, structure, and coordination needed to manage all transition workstreams. The PMO will track deliverables, manage schedules, monitor risks, and ensure seamless integration between staffing, systems, training, and governance activities. It will maintain the master transition schedule, facilitate reporting dashboards, and prepare weekly updates and performance summaries for MMSD and the Transition Leadership Team. 

By centralizing progress tracking and documentation, the PMO ensures full transparency, disciplined execution, and seamless alignment with MMSD’s governance framework and communication expectations. 

###### **1.3.5. Comprehensive Transition Support** 

As depicted in our transition team organization chart, Jacobs’ transition team includes specialized experts across all key functions. These professionals bring a disciplined yet people-centered approach to mobilization and startup. During the transition period, this team will work side-by-side with MMSD staff to ensure a smooth and efficient handoff. Their focus includes: 

- **Leading the implementation of Jacobs’ systems, tools, and standard processes** while maintaining focus on uninterrupted day-to-day operations. 

- **Coordinating onboarding and training programs** that introduce employees to Jacobs’ culture, safety principles, and operational excellence standards. 

- **Integrating asset and data management systems** to align with MMSD’s CMMS, SCADA, and reporting requirements. 

- **Providing continuity and follow-through beyond the initial handover** , remaining available to MMSD for ongoing support, optimization, and knowledge sharing. 

This hands-on leadership approach ensures that MMSD’s transition is guided by seasoned professionals who understand both the human and technical dimensions of change. By maintaining a dedicated presence throughout the process, Jacobs reduces risk, accelerates integration, and delivers a transparent and collaborative transition that reflects the professionalism and partnership MMSD expects. 

- S Despite the many challenges presented by the COVID-19 outbreak, Jacobs implemented a smooth and successful transition from our previous operator. Jacobs assembled a transition team of HR, operational, and technical professionals to implement their plan safely and efficiently. The plan included constant communication with the owner; a safe, private, and remote process for employees onboarding to Jacobs; and a technical team that applied their engineering and operational expertise to ensure that operations continued without interruption. Following a successful transition, we look forward to a continued partnership with Jacobs for many years to come. 

   - **Vincent R. Carroccia, Deputy Commissioner, Department of Public Works, City of Wilmington, DE, 302.576.3081** 



###### **1.4. Transition Training Period** 

Training is a critical bridge between mobilization and long-term operational excellence. During the transition period, Jacobs’ O&M staff will complete structured onboarding and hands-on familiarization across MMSD facilities, including the WRFs, collection system, tunnels, and pumping systems. Our objective is to ensure all employees, both transitioning and new, are fully prepared, credentialed, and confident before the March 1, 2028, Operational Start Date. 

Following onboarding, Jacobs personnel will shadow MMSD staff to capture institutional knowledge and gain firsthand understanding of process operations, data and reporting protocols, performance metrics, CMMS workflows, asset management practices, and Milorganite production. Training will cover biosolids blending, dewatering, drying, product classification, and silo storage, with a consistent focus on safety, reliability, and product quality. 

Jacobs and trusted partners such as Mead & Hunt will provide collection system expertise and work closely with MMSD’s laboratory staff to align quality systems and QA/QC procedures. All staff will complete required safety and operational training prior to startup, and role-specific development needs identified during transition will be incorporated into ongoing training plan.

###### **1.5. Mobilization Overview** 

Jacobs will implement a structured 200-point transition and mobilization plan immediately after contract award and concluding on March 1, 2028. This plan ensures all staffing, systems, governance, and readiness activities are completed before operations commence. The approach aligns with Exhibit E requirements and is anchored in Jacobs’ steps to setup a PgM framework for success, provided in **Exhibit IV-8** in **_Section IV.A.1.2.1_** . The detailed plan will be finalized jointly with MMSD at contract award. 

###### **1.6. Transition Governance and Communication** 

Jacobs will establish a three-tiered transition governance structure aligned with Exhibit P, including Executive, Operations, and Maintenance Committees. These committees will begin meeting within 10 business days of contract execution to guide implementation, ensure alignment, and maintain consistent communication between MMSD and Jacobs leadership. 

- S The transition from our former operator to Jacobs has been surprisingly pleasant. Jacobs’ staff seem exceedingly knowledgeable in all aspects of wastewater treatment and have done an exceptional job of making our plant look and run better in just a few months. 

###### **–  Firooz Fath-Azam, South Huron Valley System Manager, 734.486.2393** 



###### **1.6.1. Transition Communications Plan** 

Jacobs will implement a transition communications plan to provide transparency, clear expectations, and consistent engagement throughout the pre-term period. The plan defines stakeholder roles, meeting cadence, knowledge transfer protocols, documentation standards, and progress reporting. Key elements include: 

- Defined stakeholders and points of contact 

- Weekly progress meetings and bi-weekly governance reviews 

- Structured knowledge transfer and documentation protocols 

- A shared digital workspace for deliverables and records 

- Dashboard reporting on schedule, staffing, and risk mitigation. 

###### **1.7. Technology and CMMS Integration** 

Jacobs recognizes CMMS integration as a critical pre-term activity. Our approach is described in detail in **_Section IV.C, CMMS Approach_** . 

###### **1.8. Integrated Collection and Treatment System Modeling and System Readiness** 

During the transition period, Jacobs will collaborate with MMSD to evaluate the operation of the ISS and WRFs as an integrated system, particularly under increasingly intense rainfall conditions. Coordination with Storm Harvester will support understanding of system behavior before, during, and after significant storm events. Additional details are provided in **_Section IV.A, 2.2.1 Collection System, Tunnels, and Wet Weather Flow Management Focus Area_** . 

###### **1.9. Assurance of Continuity** 

Through proven systems, experienced personnel, and local familiarity, we’ll maintain continuity across MMSD’s facilities and services throughout the transition. Drawing on lessons learned from dozens of complex treatment and conveyance transitions, Jacobs will deliver a seamless, non-disruptive handover that is reassuring to employees and invisible to the public. 

###### **1.10. Schedule for Smooth Transition of Operations and Related Systems** 

Jacobs’ transition approach is governed by a clearly defined, phased schedule that ensures all operational, staffing, and system milestones are achieved before March 1, 2028. While the Draft Contract does not specify a transition duration, Jacobs has developed an approximately 18-month pre-term schedule consistent with §3.02 (Mobilization and Staffing Transition) and §3.03(a) (CMMS Implementation). 

The phased schedule emphasizes early engagement, structured onboarding, retention of institutional knowledge, and flexibility to deploy additional expertise as needed. Each phase is supported by Jacobs’ governance and communication framework, providing MMSD with transparency, accountability, and confidence in progress. The accompanying high-level schedule ( **Exhibit IV-58** ) illustrates how key activities will be sequenced and monitored to ensure full operational readiness. 

All transition activities, decisions, and milestones will be documented and accessible, supporting a smooth progression to the operational start date.

###### C. Computerized Maintenance Management System (CMMS) Approach 



###### 1. JACOBS’ DIFFERENTIATING STRENGTH IN CMMS IMPLEMENTATION 

Jacobs offers MMSD a capability unmatched by any other proposer. As a fully integrated firm, we offer O&M, digital and data solutions, engineering, and asset management expertise. As discussed in **_Section A.iii Maintenance Approach_** , we align our approach to industry standards such as the Society of Maintenance and Reliability Professionals, IAM, ISO 55000, and PAS55 and consider each client’s unique needs and requirements. This unique combination of skills allows MMSD to benefit from a partner that not only operates and maintains major wastewater systems but also designs, configures, and optimizes CMMS platforms for utilities worldwide and works with software companies and implementors to ensure system configurations meet organizational goals. 

Jacobs will collaborate with MMSD and NEXGEN’s implementation team to: 

- Configure a unified system covering both WRFs and conveyance assets. 

- Align PM and CM workflows with MMSD’s Maintenance Resource Guide and governance requirements. 

- Integrate CMMS data with MMSD and Jacobs systems for complete operational visibility and PdM systems to provide escalations to intercept issues. 

- Develop dashboards and analytics that provide real-time insight into asset performance, staffing, and costs. 

###### JACOBS BRINGS PROVEN NEXGEN IMPLEMENTATION EXPERIENCE 

Jacobs’ consulting and technology teams have implemented or managed dozens of large-scale CMMS technology solutions—many using MMSD’s chosen software, NEXGEN CMMS. We are experienced with NEXGEN’s implementation and training process. We understand how its technology solution integrates with SCADA, GIS, and laboratory systems, how its data architecture is configured, and how to leverage its full capabilities to drive measurable results. Our experience with MMSD’s prior SCADA modernization and conveyance risk assessments give us firsthand knowledge of MMSD’s digital ecosystem, positioning us to help MMSD realize immediate and sustained value from NEXGEN. 

The NEXGEN CMMS is required to be online by March 1, 2028. Jacobs has a proven track record of successful implementations with NEXGEN and can work together effectively and efficiently to meet that deadline. 

###### 2. CURRENT CONTEXT AND PARTNERSHIP FRAMEWORK 

MMSD’s new NEXGEN CMMS will become the foundation for your asset management, operations, and maintenance transparency goals. It’s the digital backbone of your asset management activities, with primary functionality that will support the line-of-sight framework that drives effective maintenance delivery. Data entered into NEXGEN is used to inform asset reliability, O&M optimization, rapid response to corrective issues, optimized inventory management, planning and scheduling, regulatory performance, asset risk management, and lifecycle forecasting. Consequently, it’s critical to understand desired data reporting output needs, during system configuration. Jacobs can provide input on that process.

###### D. Potential Additive Work - Level 3 PdM 

###### 1. JACOBS UNDERSTANDS AND AGREES WITH MMSD’S GOALS FOR PdM 

Today’s PdM environment at MMSD is fragmented. Multiple vendors provide vibration, oil, and OEM-specific analytics, but results are not consistently visible, comparable, or actionable. MMSD recognizes that its current data architecture does not yet support asset-level traceability of condition, work history, and failure modes—limiting the value of predictive analytics. MMSD is therefore seeking a PdM program that consolidates disparate data sources, removes black-box vendor dependencies, strengthens data quality, and produces reliable, automated insights tied to specific assets and components. 

Jacobs understands that MMSD wants a **coherent, transparent, and scalable strategy** for PdM—one that begins with the assets of highest consequence and expands as value is demonstrated. MMSD expects Level 3 PdM to be priced separately from the base O&M fee, consistent with the RFP, and to leverage its existing infrastructure rather than adding burdensome hardware or software. The program must align with MMSD’s criticality rankings, integrate analytics into Jacobs’ workflows, and support MMSD’s formal governance bodies through clear reporting and structured decision-making. 

Jacobs’ approach reflects MMSD’s goals: PdM architecture that builds on MMSD’s current systems, elevates the value of data they already collect, and adds targeted advanced technologies where they create meaningful gains in reliability, cost efficiency, and operational resilience. 

###### 2. SUMMARY OF LEVELS 1–3 PdM 

**Level 1 – MMSD-Owned Data and Analytics.** Level 1 consists of asset and operational data collected and monitored directly by MMSD—primarily SCADA, historian trends, alarms, and operator observations. While not priced as PdM work, these datasets are essential inputs that Jacobs will incorporate into Level 3 analytics. Level 1 data provides the operational context needed to interpret condition signatures, identify trends, and refine predictive thresholds. 

**Level 2 – Jacobs’ Standard PdM/PM (Included in Base Fee).** Level 2 includes Jacobs’ traditional predictive and preventive technologies: handheld vibration readings, infrared thermography, oil and tribology analysis, static electrical testing, and vendor/OEM-prescribed PM activities. MMSD classifies these activities as PM, and they are fully included in the base O&M price. Level 2 provides measurable condition indicators and early warnings and serves as a foundation on which Level 3 is built. 



<!-- Start of picture text -->
Beneft to MMSD of an Integrated PdM Approach :<br>� Operational reliability improves as failure modes are detected earlier<br>and equipment health is continuously monitored.<br>� Operational risk diminishes, particularly for high-consequence assets.<br>� Maintenance costs trend downward as unplanned outages and<br>emergency responses are minimized.<br>� Energy efficiency improves through early detection and correction of<br>electrical and hydraulic inefficiencies.<br>� Asset and capital planning decisions become more informed, supported by<br>quantifiable condition data and demonstrated performance trends.<br>� Just as importantly, the program creates transparency and trust.<br>� MMSD will no longer rely on vendor black boxes or fragmented PdM<br>reporting. Instead, PdM insights become part of MMSD’s everyday<br>maintenance workflow, governed through established committees and<br>fully visible in NEXGEN. 367_007CAM_1<br><!-- End of picture text -->

**Level 3 – Advanced, Real-Time, Integrated PdM (Additive Scope).** Level 3 PdM is an additive, on-demand scope that integrates continuous monitoring technologies, automated diagnostics, and NEXGEN-triggered workflows. Rather than relying on periodic measurements, Level 3 delivers **continuous or high-frequency analytics** that detect subtle changes in equipment behavior long before failure modes develop. Tools such as VibeCloud (real-time vibration and temperature), Artesis (motor current signature analysis and sensorless pump performance analytics), and complementary technologies lik **e Sewer Line Rapid Assessment Tool (SL-RAT)** for acoustic inspection provide early insight into mechanical, electrical, and hydraulic degradation. 

The distinguishing characteristic of Level 3 is not the sensor alone—it's the automated analysis, continuous diagnostics, and integration with NEXGEN to generate actionable work orders. Level 3 unifies MMSD’s Level 1 and Level 2 data with new real-time insights to form a complete, predictive view of asset health.

###### 3. CURRENT PdM AT MMSD 

MMSD already performs important PdM activities, but they remain siloed across multiple vendor platforms. Vibration and oil analyses cover more than 200 assets, while OEM systems for equipment such as Solar Turbines, Caterpillar engines, and Bentley aeration provide additional diagnostic insights. Although these datasets contain valuable information, they are not consistently integrated into maintenance decision-making. Reports may not be reviewed in real time, and corrective work is not always tied to specific assets within the CMMS. 

Today, many maintenance activities are recorded at higher-level locations rather than at the individual asset or component level. This limits MMSD’s ability to correlate PdM findings with past work, trend condition data over time, or identify systemic degradation patterns. A key benefit of the Level 3 PdM program is the opportunity to strengthen asset-level documentation within NEXGEN, improving the visibility, traceability, and long-term value of PdM insights. 

Jacobs’ Level 3 PdM program addresses these challenges directly by consolidating all available PdM inputs, strengthening data discipline, and making insights visible and actionable through NEXGEN. 

###### 4. JACOBS’ RECOMMENDED LEVEL 3 PdM PLAN 

Jacobs recommends a suite of proven, cost-effective PdM technologies that complement MMSD’s existing systems and provide actionable, real-time insights for the treatment facilities and conveyance system assets. 

###### **4.1. Proposed PdM Technologies for MMSD’s Treatment Facilities** 

Illustrated in **Exhibit IV-60** , our recommended Level 3 PdM technologies for the WRFs include: 

- **Continuous vibration and temperature monitoring (VibeCloud or Augury)** will provide continuous insight into the mechanical condition of rotating equipment by measuring changes in vibration amplitude, frequency signatures, and thermal behavior. These sensors detect early signs of imbalance, misalignment, bearing wear, lubrication issues, and electrical or mechanical stress long before they affect performance. Continuous monitoring offers a far richer understanding of equipment behavior than periodic handheld readings and enables automated diagnostics to identify trends, anomalies, and emerging failure modes under real operating conditions. When integrated with SCADA and NEXGEN CMMS, vibration and temperature monitoring systems can generate condition-based alerts and work orders, strengthen asset health scoring, and support a proactive, PdM strategy that improves reliability and reduces unplanned downtime. 

- **Artesis – motor current signature analysis (MCSA) and sensorless pump performance analytics** will deliver high-resolution electrical signature analysis that detects mechanical, electrical, and power-quality–related degradation within motors and pump systems. This MCSA approach identifies bearing issues, rotor defects, stator problems, and load-related inefficiencies long before they manifest as operational failures. Artesis also offers a sensorless pump performance capability that compares real-time operating conditions to ideal pump curves, enabling utilities to identify hydraulic inefficiencies and energy waste. These insights can be integrated with NEXGEN to automatically generate investigative work orders when deviations occur. We would work collaboratively with MMSD to prioritize the most critical assets where Artesis would provide the greatest value based on asset condition and risk scoring.

� **Infrared/thermal Imaging (infrared thermography)** identifies thermal patterns associated with electrical resistance, loose connections, overloaded circuits, and mechanical stress. It is particularly valuable for motor control centers, switchgear, and rotating equipment. By regularly trending thermal profiles, MMSD can detect risk conditions long before they lead to electrical faults or mechanical degradation. Thermal imaging complements both vibration and MCSA data by providing a non-contact indicator of emerging failure modes. 

� **Enhanced oil and tribology analysis** provides insight into internal wear mechanisms, lubricant degradation, contamination, and overall equipment health. MMSD already performs oil sampling, but under the Level 3 PdM program, Jacobs will integrate these samples into a broader asset condition framework, correlating oil characteristics with vibration, thermal, and MCSA insights. This strengthens diagnostics, improves trend reliability, and enhances predictive accuracy for gearboxes, motors, blowers, and turbine systems. 

Based on our analysis of the WRFs’ assets, we’ve identified and prioritized the types of assets where continuous monitoring is most beneficial based on asset criticality, as shown in **Exhibit IV-61** . We’ll work with MMSD to confirm the final selection of assets for Level 3 PdM deployment.

###### **4.2. Proposed PdM Technologies for MMSD’s Conveyance System** 

###### **4.3. Additional PdM Technologies Included in Our Base Fee** 

**AquaDNA Deragger** is an intelligent pump optimization technology that actively prevents ragging and blockage events by automatically adjusting pump operation to clear debris before it accumulates. Installed at high-consequence conveyance pump stations, the Deragger improves hydraulic performance, reduces pump starts and energy use associated with rag-induced inefficiency, and minimizes emergency callouts and maintenance interventions. For MMSD, Jacobs proposes deploying AquaDNA Deraggers at two jointly selected pump stations as a proof of concept, targeting locations with a history of clogging or elevated operational risk. Integrating this technology into the Level 3 PdM framework allows pump performance anomalies to be detected earlier and addressed proactively, enhancing reliability across the conveyance system. See **Exhibit IV-62** for an example dashboard. 

Beyond the recommended Level 3 PdM technologies listed above, we’ve included two Level 3 PdM technologies as part of our Base Fee. We intend to implement them as part of our value-added innovations discussed above in **Exhibit IV-52** under **_Section 5.3 Value-Added Improvements and Innovations Included as Part of the Base Fee._** These two technologies will support more cost-effective maintenance of the conveyance system and include: 

- **InfoSense SL-RAT** uses acoustics, rather than CCTV or cleaning, to rapidly assess blockages in gravity sewer lines. A transmitter and receiver placed at adjacent manholes measure sound transmission to determine whether the pipe segment is clear, partially obstructed, or likely blocked. The tool requires only two operators, minimal setup time, and no confined-space entry. SL-RAT helps MMSD prioritize cleaning, reduce unnecessary Vactor truck deployments, and lower overall conveyance maintenance costs. 

###### EXHIBIT IV-62. JACOBS’ AquaDNA “DERAGGER” SYSTEM 

� **Dragonfly and ArgonLite Supplemental Sewer Inspection Technologies** are

supplemental inspection tools designed for small-diameter or hard-to-access sewer segments where traditional CCTV or acoustic assessments may be impractical. These lightweight, portable systems capture visual and structural information within short pipe runs, providing additional insight into localized defects, debris accumulation, infiltration, or condition anomalies. While cost and deployment considerations are still being evaluated, Dragonfly and ArgonLite can complement MMSD’s acoustic SL-RAT assessments by offering targeted visual confirmation or refined condition detail wh ~~e~~ re needed. Used selectiv ~~e~~ ly within the Level 3 PdM framework, these tools can enhance conveyance risk assessment and support more efficient maintenance planning. See **Exhibit IV-63** for examples of this innovative defect coding tool and data-driven recommendations dashboard.

This program is designed to **build on MMSD’s existing systems** . D. Potential Additive Work - Level 3 PDM 

IV. APPROACH 

###### 8. INTEGRATION WITH SCADA AND NEXGEN CMMS 

Successful Level 3 PdM depends on tight integration between predictive technologies, SCADA, the SCADA historian, and NEXGEN. Jacobs will configure SCADA points and data flows for PdM inputs, ensuring alignment with MMSD’s OT architecture and cybersecurity requirements. PdM tools will route their analytics to the historian or directly into NEXGEN via open APIs, where automated work orders will be generated based on agreed thresholds. 

NEXGEN will be configured to accept condition levels, alerts, and asset health scores and to associate them with specific assets. This will improve work planning, enhance prioritization, and eliminate the manual interpretation of PdM reports that currently burdens MMSD.

###### 9. CRITICAL CONSIDERATIONS IN DEVELOPING JACOBS’ LEVEL 3 PdM APPROACH 

Jacobs’ Level 3 PdM plan clearly differentiates between Level 2 (base scope) and Level 3 (additive) activities, avoiding any risk of double counting. All PdM procedures, thresholds, and condition ratings will be documented in NEXGEN as required in Exhibit B. Data routing, access control, and API integrations will comply with Exhibit Q cybersecurity requirements. Pricing for Level 3 PdM will follow the RFP requirements, including an itemized breakdown for PdM as required in Pricing Worksheet Item J.

## STATEMENT ON REGIONAL PARTNERSHIP 

###### **Jacobs looks forward to collaborating with MMSD to develop creative regional partnership strategies that will foster community trust and provide economic growth opportunities for local and regional residents and businesses.** 

S This O&M contract represents far more than the operation and maintenance of MMSD’s sewerage infrastructure. It is a long-term commitment by Jacobs and our entire proposed team to serve as a fully integrated partner—one aligned with MMSD’s culture, priorities, and mission. Together, we are dedicated to protecting public health and safety, prioritizing the communities we serve, and preserving and enhancing the environment for future generations. 

Jacobs’ regional partnership strategies will contribute to equity, sustainability, and economic growth for MMSD’s service area and regional communities. We’ll support and complement the outreach and regional initiatives that MMSD is currently spearheading (community engagement, workforce development, small business and supplier development, and research and development). While we view these activities as critical, we’ve also proposed above and beyond ideas that will catalyze partnerships and produce measurable regional benefits. 

###### A. Community-Focused Leadership Team to Drive Positive Long-Term Outcomes 



Our regional partnership leadership team offers a strong local focus. The caliber of their experience demonstrates our commitment to provide the resources and structure to ensure transparency, accountability, and collaboration, and to align with MMSD’s 2035 Vision: 

- **Rick Warner** is a Milwaukee resident with extensive experience managing water infrastructure programs. He brings a strong track record of forging partnerships in the water industry, and excels at connecting municipalities, workforce leaders, and community organizations to deliver sustainable solutions. 

- **Toyin Ogunfolaju** is a government relations and social value specialist who has led initiatives that have embedded sustainable social, economic, and workforce solutions into major infrastructure programs. Her focus is on prioritizing equity and local talent development, drawing on her expertise developing workforce development programs, advancing workforce inclusion, and building partnerships with communities, educational institutions, and local organizations. 

###### **– Greg Fischer, President of OMI (Jacobs’ O&M Group)** 

- **Megan Myszka** brings experience in stakeholder engagement and public involvement for major infrastructure projects. She has managed outreach for complex, high-profile initiatives and infrastructure plans, and excels at developing inclusive public involvement and communication strategies aimed at building trust with diverse communities.

- **Sustainability and Resilience:** You’re positioned, via the 2035 Vision and 20252027 Strategic Plan, to be a national model for climate resilience and sustainable water management through integrated watershed planning, renewable energy goals, green infrastructure (GI), and promoting equity. 

True to your 2035 Vision, MMSD is doing tremendous work to improve community health and the environment, build the next generation of water professionals, and become a national model for climate resilience and sustainable water management: 

   - **Research:** You’re investing in a $13M clean-water research lab at the South Shore facility and piloting innovative technologies such as the Proteus Upflow Media Filter and GI Performance Testing. And you’re implementing a research program to explore new ways to optimize operational performance. 

- **Community Health and Environment:** As a sustainability leader, you’re cutting greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions and working with community partners to advance climate resilience and resource recovery.  You're taking a proactive role in preventing flooding and restoring habitat with such projects as the Kinnickinnic River Corridor transformation and taking every opportunity to also revitalize neighborhoods with public amenities as you do so. 

We’re inspired by your vision—and by all your ongoing initiatives, which we plan to support. **Exhibit V-4** presents our ideas for furthering your goals, building on the foundation you’ve established, to go “above and beyond” and then maybe even “aiming higher.” **Exhibit V-3** is one of those “above and beyond” ideas. 

- **Workforce and Economic Development:** You’re working hard to build the pipeline of water professionals through internships, training, and career pathways through such programs as Fresh Coast Ambassador & Fresh Start, as well as your on-thejob training partnership with Employ Milwaukee and the Water Equity Task Force. 



###### EXHIBIT V-3. FISHING PIER AND RIVER ACCESS CONCEPT 

###### **Leveraging an Ongoing Riverfront Estuary Renewal to Enhance Regional Benefits** 

Jacobs proposes to advance a Fishing Pier and River Access concept tied to the ongoing EPA sediment cleanup on the Milwaukee River, in partnership with MMSD. Building on our role in the $450M Milwaukee Estuary restoration, we would help align MMSD, the City, County Parks, the Urban Ecology Center, and private partners to assess and deliver this amenity. Our support would include feasibility study, design, permitting, construction oversight, funding support, and stakeholder coordination. By pairing river access with sediment cleanup and habitat restoration, MMSD can expand public use of the river while demonstrating leadership in environmental stewardship and community partnership. Our vision is captured below:

###### C. Examples - Serving as an Engaged Regional Partner in Other Communities with Similar Contracts 



Beyond facility walls and project boundaries, our purpose is to create a more connected, sustainable world. We act as community stewards and partners wherever we go—from O&M contracts for water reclamation facilities to large-scale infrastructure improvements. What follows are examples of where we served as an engaged regional partner for contracts with similarities to MMSD’s operating environment and values, as well as where we’ve helped improve the surrounding communities. 



###### 1. JXN Water, Jackson, Mississippi 

_Focused on public health, equity, transparency, and long-term stewardship while investing in the community and maintaining compliance and workforce solutions._ 

Since assuming operations from Veolia in the wake of the 2022 crisis that left residents without safe drinking 

water, Jacobs has been stabilizing and modernizing Jackson’s water services. Based on our success, in 2025, JXN Water awarded Jacobs a 9-year contract to operate and maintain its wastewater treatment facilities. We’re investing in community initiatives, workforce development, and environmental stewardship, helping develop a local workforce pipeline to support long-term system reliability. We: 

- Partnered with Hinds Community College to deliver Water and Maintenance 101 training, host career fairs, conduct facility tours, and donated to the JXN Water Scholarship Fund, which will cover full tuition for two students focused on water-related careers. 

- Created pathways for existing entry-level staff to gain additional technical training through a free custom college course. 

- Established an engineering scholarship endowment at Jackson State University (JSU), matched by the university, providing perpetual support to students. 

- Hired **six local interns across operations, maintenance, and engineering—three interns graduated and joined Jacobs as full-time staff** . 

- Provided career 101 modules at National Society of Black Engineers events at JSU, a historically black college/university, established a $25K endowment scholarship for engineering students, matched by a $25K donation from JSU. 

- In addition to our O&M role, we’re the Lead and Copper Rule Compliance Program Manager for JXN Water. In advance of the upcoming Lead and Copper Rule Improvement, we partnered with the Mississippi State Extension service to provide early tap sampling and remediation to schools and childcare facilities in our distribution network through their “SipSafe” Program. We crafted materials and hosted informational sessions to help facilities understand upcoming sampling requirements and what they can do to keep their students safe. We helped bridge the gap between SipSafe and the Mississippi Low Income Childcare Initiative to help facilities across the state understand what’s required from them and provide opportunities for testing. 



2.  City of San Marcos, Texas

- Staff regularly participates in community work, including the Great Texas River Cleanup, involving 700 community volunteers.
